the rapporteur. - mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the report i have the honour of presenting to the house refers to a communication from the commission to the council and parliament on the upgrading of the 1993 investment services directive. the commission has published its communication in the context of the broader action plan for financial services, drawn up in implementation of the lisbon european council decisions on the integration of european money and capital markets. the integration of these markets is part of the completion of the internal market. the last stockholm european council returned to the subject, calling for the action plan to be fully implemented by 2005 and for all interested parties to make every effort to achieve a unified securities market by the end of 2003. investment services are certainly an important tool, perhaps the most important for the functioning of securities markets. it is clear that every initiative in this field, such as the one contained in the commission communication which i have the honour to present to you, has been given the stamp of urgency by the european council. the commission communication is not a legislative text. it is, however, of the utmost importance because it provides a preliminary basis for the necessary reforms so that when, i believe, the legislative proposal is due to be tabled, parliament will be able to adopt the necessary amendments to the original directive quickly and, i hope, almost unanimously. i think that we can also give an example of the speed and efficiency of our legislative process, which has unfortunately been called into question recently by certain reports such as the lamfalussy report. the socialist group's objective in economic policy is not, and has never been, simply to redistribute income and wealth more fairly and to reduce inequalities in society. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. we believe that progress in these areas will strengthen the productive potential of the european economy for the benefit of all. we also believe that the changes needed to achieve this cannot be brought about by the market through its own autonomous and automatic processes. the spontaneous development of market institutions often traps the economy in counterproductive situations, creates monopolies and safeguards the vested interests of a few individuals at the expense of society. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one. i have the honour to inform you of such reform measures in this report on the upgrading of the directive. the directive was originally intended in 1993 to promote market integration by combating national protectionism in the provision of financial services. the central idea was the mutual recognition by all member states of the authorizations granted by any member state to credit institutions, the famous european passport. the idea was brilliant, but it was largely cancelled out in practice by the possibility for member states to impose operational rules on the provision of these services separately. the fact that after the original directive the financial company no longer had to submit 15 applications and obtain 15 licences, but only one licence to establish itself throughout europe, was undoubtedly progress, but it was losing much of its power because in the course of its operation the same company was obliged to deal with 15 different legal regimes. this situation has acted as a major national regulatory barrier to the unimpeded flow of financial services, to the integration of european markets. i now propose, and i am pleased that the rapporteur for the people's party, my colleague mrs kauppi, agrees with this proposal, that we remove this barrier by imposing the application of a single regulatory regime, which will be the regime of the member state granting the licence, instead of 15. the integration of certain aspects of the trading activity, and in particular the integration of clearing and settlement, of securities transactions, will also be a major step towards the completion of the single market. in the united states, which has much to teach us on these matters, there is only one central clearing house for the whole country. in europe we have many more than one. since it is possible to achieve large economies of scale in clearing services and thus reduce the costs for the parties involved, which are considerably higher in the united states than in the united kingdom, the directive should facilitate the integration of such functions. i would like to say that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the proposed merger, but it is not yet possible to say whether the merger will be carried out in the future. by thus enhancing competition in the merger process promoted by the euro, we can hope that the companies with the lowest costs will eventually prevail in consolidation rather than the companies with the strongest monopoly links. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented in a uniform manner. today, regulated markets have lost their character as infrastructure bodies and have become commercial enterprises, while many large financial companies, equipped with electronic means, have been transformed from market intermediaries into internal securities markets. the regulatory rules should also be adapted accordingly. many of the rules on market integrity, such as the fight against insider dealing, should be extended to companies for their internal transactions, while other rules that once applied only to companies, such as customer relations rules, should also apply to stock exchanges. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the rules on the free movement of goods are applied in the member states, and i would like to ask the commissioner to give us a few more details. in conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to stress that the socialist group supports the integration of the financial markets with a view to strengthening the investments necessary to achieve full employment in europe. it certainly does not support, and certainly does not oppose, the speculative fever of the stock markets, which can create explosive catastrophic situations for the real economy. we therefore ask the commission to present its proposal as soon as possible to safeguard the integrity of the markets. we are certainly not deluding ourselves into thinking that it is possible for a regulation to eliminate speculation. but we want at least the principles of commercial honesty to be strengthened, precisely because because of speculation the temptations to dishonest action are growing, while the stock exchanges are increasingly strengthening their position in the pension functioning of our societies. the conditions under which european stock exchanges will be fed by workers' pension funds cannot but be much stricter than those of the time when stock exchanges were simply a casino for the rich. the strengthening of stock exchange discipline and the proper functioning of stock exchanges is one of the most important projects in the financial markets action plan.

- mr president, the latest developments in foot-and-mouth disease in a number of european union countries show that the systems for ensuring effective control of animal diseases are in an unacceptable state. this is due to the demand of the european union's multinational enterprises for complete freedom of movement of goods within the community, a demand that is being realised by the establishment of the single internal market. the european union has also introduced a system of veterinary checks on animals and animal products moving between the member states. this has created the possibility of easy transmission and the difficult if not impossible control of diseases from country to country. at the same time, the certification of the health status of animals and animal products in transit is more symbolic than substantive. this is evidenced by the unacceptable image given today by veterinary services which are not even rudimentarily organised to meet the higher quantitative and qualitative requirements of the single internal market. this is the natural consequence of the multinationals' demand for the removal of all obstacles and the minimisation of controls, which are essentially delegated to the companies themselves, as has been demonstrated by the other recent problems with dioxins, spongiform encephalopathy, etc. the present situation is therefore the price of the policy of establishing the single internal market and the requirements for ever fewer controls. this shows the need to reintroduce border controls within the community in order to prevent the emergence of generalized epidemics and the need to compensate farmers for the damage they have suffered. the compensation must not be linked to cuts in community funds for other agricultural products, much less for countries which bear no responsibility for the appearance and scale of the present problem.

the rapporteur. - mr president, the health programme we are voting on is the first integrated european union project in this field. it is timed to coincide with the great development of information gathering and dissemination technology and the growing awareness of european citizens of the quality of life, which is catalytically affected by health protection. the new programme adopts a comprehensive approach to health protection and to the means, mechanisms and practices for dealing with health problems. the programme thus sets three objectives, which are to be achieved by close cooperation between the member states in a comprehensive and two-way manner. the first objective is to collect, process and disseminate medical data, information, practices and knowledge from all sources. the second objective is to develop the community's mechanism for a direct, rapid and coordinated response to health threats. objective 3: tackling the determinants of health linked to lifestyle, socioeconomic conditions or the environment. the study of these objectives will lead to the formulation of strategy and policies to ensure the quality of individual actions and to make health protection more effective. however, the major weakness of the programme was that it lacked a coordination centre which was absolutely necessary in an effort so extensive and complex involving the national services of the member states, non-governmental organisations, international bodies, commission departments, networks of existing programmes, networks to be developed as proposed in the programme, and many others. i have paid particular attention to this deficiency and, after study and a series of consultations, i have come to the proposal for the establishment of a health coordination and monitoring centre, which is proposed to operate within the framework of the community, that is, the european commission, and within the programme with the help of a committee composed of representatives of the member states. this is the best way of ensuring that all the objectives are achieved. the coordination element has been supplemented by a number of features referring to the introduction of an integrated health strategy through the development of cross-sectoral policy in the definition and implementation of all community policies and actions. the programme theme on addressing the determinants of health is proposed to be supplemented by the development of strategies and measures for genetic, prenatal and biological factors that clearly affect health. support for actions to identify and define good health practices and guidelines is also a key element in ensuring a high level of health protection and the prevention of human disease. it is clear that without the comparability of information, the compatibility of data and the interoperability of the systems and networks being developed or to be developed under the programme, the impact of the actions would be limited and community action would not be effective. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to cover the protection of personal data. the other aspect which i consider very important is the budget. there must always be a correspondence between the objectives one sets and the means one mobilizes to achieve them. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the programme is implemented in a way that is compatible with the common market. the commission has also taken steps to ensure that the programme is implemented in a way that is compatible with the common market. in view of the above, amendments are made to allow the objectives of the programme to be achieved. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work is a step in the right direction, and it is a step in the right direction. mr president, this programme comes at the most opportune time, and in particular at the time of the enormous development of information technology together with the astonishing achievements of molecular biology, genetics and biotechnology. these will fundamentally change preventive medicine and therefore be decisive in preventing disease. in conclusion, i would like to say that strengthening the foundations of the european construction requires convergence not only in the strategic and political fields of the economy and foreign and security policy, but also in individual areas such as health, education and others. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i therefore consider that the new programme is the beginning of such a process of convergence in the vital field of public health.
- mr president, we are discussing today, at first reading, one of the most important issues of concern to european citizens, the action programme on public health. for the first time, it presents the development of european health policy, highlights its achievements and weaknesses, and highlights the difficulties in implementing a coherent community policy. the european parliament, by adopting the report, must demonstrate its commitment to health promotion, the precautionary principle and the improvement of the provision of medical services at all levels. it is clear that areas such as research mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i am not sure constantine was byzantine, after all he was borderline between byzantium and the roman empire. were it justinian, of course, it would have been a different matter. anyway the issue we are addressing tonight is the extent to which a member state is permitted under a treaty to place restrictions upon nationals of other member states or its own nationals, i suppose, from conducting intra-eu investment within the sphere of its economy. this matter is particularly relevant to investments in privatisation conducted by member states. the issue is of fundamental importance for the correct functioning of the european single market and i would like to thank colleagues for raising the subject this time given the number of cases the commission has referred to the court of justice over recent years. the treaty establishing the european communities under article 43 on right of establishment and article 56, as mentioned by commissioner bolkestein, on free movement of capital, clearly creates a regime whereby nationals of any member state have the right to invest in other member states. this is a right that a very large number of individuals and enterprises have availed themselves of as the european union has developed. intra-eu investment has been increasing by staggering proportions over the last few years. in 1998, such investment totalled over eur 132 billion; in 1999, it had increased by over 80% to over eur 240 billion. we can reasonably expect that these figures will be easily surpassed and the rate of increase maintained as the establishment of the euro reduces intra-community transactions, eliminates exchange risk and exchange cost for enterprises whilst at the same time increasing intra-community investment opportunities. such is the success of the european economic project and the establishment of the euro. however, one can always seek improvements. there are still many tasks for the completion of the european single market and one of these is, of course, the gradual dismantling of state involvement in enterprises. this dismantling has only been pursued with great political courage and with grave political risks. often it has only been achieved through the legal assurances that governments gave about their continued supervision and special interests in the strategic directions of the enterprises being privatised. such legal assurances came in many forms, such as the golden share, so that there is a case, i suppose, to argue that the golden share has not always been an impediment but may have actually helped politically the privatisation process. in its 1997 communication, the commission established its guidelines for the existence of these legal assurances. it did so in a rather non-transparent way through a communication that was not discussed either in parliament or by the council. the legal assurances that governments had created, on the other hand, were established in the full glare of publicity and public debate that necessarily exists in the privatisation process. it really is rather an uncomfortable situation that such a dichotomy has been created between the establishment of fundamental community rules and member state regulations. the commission, using the communication as its operational guide, has since taken legal action against six member states before the court of justice and opened infringement proceedings against the others. none of these cases has, as yet, come to a conclusion. it cannot be justified that the commission has set up its own framework of rules under which it has been bringing actions against member states, which have gone through enormous difficulties in bringing forward necessary privatisation programmes. i agree with the commission, when it states that the national interest argument used to justify such legal assurances by member states, as has frequently happened, cannot be accepted as an adequate justification for such measures. the national interest argument is simply not sufficiently transparent since it can cover a particularly wide range of economic and/or non-economic criteria. i can, however, also agree with those of our colleagues who argue that the commission too has been acting in a particularly non-transparent manner with regard to breaking down the barriers for intra-eu investment. i join with them in requesting that the commission update its policy in this area with a proposal for a directive, not only for the sake of intra-institutional transparency, but also to provide guidance to member states so that lengthy procedures before the court can be avoided.
the rapporteur. - mr. president, i agree with you. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the necessary measures.
in writing. - (el) recent events and their very serious consequences for the health of citizens and the food chain, such as the 'mad cow', dioxins, the depleted uranium bombs dropped on yugoslavia, and many others, make us very reserved about the supposed interest and the avalanche of proclamations and good intentions of the eu regarding public health. it is now well established that the main purpose of any eu intervention is to alleviate concerns and not to tackle the problems it creates among broad sections of the population. the eu's intentions in the particularly sensitive area of public health are also clear from the conclusions of the recent stockholm european council. thus, while the attack on a whole series of vital workers' achievements (cutting down expenditure, budgetary discipline, individual responsibility, etc.) is foreshadowed, at the same time what is commonly called 'the modernisation of the european social model' is announced, which in the health sector is reflected in a weakening of its public character and a strengthening of individual responsibility to meet needs, to the benefit of private initiative and the powerful economic interests operating in this field and in that of the insurance systems. with this action programme in the field of health for the period 2001-2006, the eu is responding to the objectives and needs of big business and monopolies in this respect. it is not by chance that nowhere is the obligation of the state for any sector or aspect of the related issues mentioned or prescribed. this eu strategy subjects health to the laws of the market, to the logic of cost, efficiency and profitability. health systems are now seen as an expense on national budgets - not as an investment in the human factor - which must be accommodated by budgetary discipline. this, in the usual community language, means a reduction in public expenditure on health, a transfer of responsibility and cost to the citizens themselves, a strengthening of private initiative, turning health care into a lucrative business. the aim is to convert health into a 'competitive market' and to ensure more favourable conditions for the penetration and conquest of it by powerful monopolies, together with the levelling down of workers' social rights and gains and the reduction of public expenditure in this sector. the rapporteur of the european parliament shares the commission's view. thus, while it does speak pertinently of the serious health problems that continue to arise, it does not speak at all of the real social causes and conditions that undermine the health and threaten the lives of citizens, such as the worsening of working conditions, shift work and night work, the dramatic deterioration in the living standards of the working masses, the spread of poverty and unemployment, the impacts of market liberalisation on the food chain in the name of profit without limits and without barriers, environmental degradation, etc. 'health cannot be considered a marketable good and, therefore, cannot be compromised at political or financial level,' the rapporteur notes, and on this we are in full agreement. unfortunately, it is denying its good intentions by endorsing and supporting the anti-social and aggressive public health policy of the eu. for these reasons we will not vote on the report, standing with the workers and helping to organise the front of their resistance and struggle to demand a genuine high-level public health service that serves the citizens.
in writing. - (el) the european commission's proposal is inspired by the usual ideology of the ruling class with a view to allegedly ensuring equal opportunities and free access for disabled people, as is clear from the rest of the title of the report in question: "towards a barrier-free europe for disabled people". the concern to conceal the social aspects of the problem is evident. what equality of opportunity can a disabled child from a poor background have with a child from a rich family under capitalism? this concealment is not accidental: all the positive measures advocated in the report (standardisation of technical aids, development of new services, etc.) will be mainly for the benefit of disabled people from the wealthy classes. finally, the commercialisation of the needs of the disabled is of interest in all areas such as education, the market for technical aids, the provision of cheap labour, etc. but various positive measures, such as access to transport, the introduction of a free movement card in the eu member states, the transfer of allowances from one country to another, do not change the above observation. the disabled are the first and most tragic victims of the policy of reducing social spending in the field of health and welfare, and of the liquidation of social security schemes. it is symptomatic that the importance of social spending to address the problems of disabled people is not highlighted in any eu text. once again, the so-called non-governmental organizations, volunteering and the family are being called upon to deal with the very acute social problems of the disabled. although there is much talk of new services, technical aids, etc., there is no mention of providing them free of charge or even of a premium for their purchase. the only thing that matters is subsidizing employers if they agree to hire a disabled person. however, although these employment assistance programmes have been in existence for years, unemployment among disabled people is close to 70%, according to studies by independent bodies published on various occasions. as regards specific training and education, apart from certain generally positive proposals which concern primarily higher education but are not binding, the text is imbued with the anti-scientific ideology in favour of the schooling of the disabled in ordinary institutions in the name of their alleged integration into society. the authors of the report are of course aware that this attempt failed in germany and france, where a series of specialised schools were closed in the early 1980s and reopened at the end of the decade to avoid social explosions. how can the deaf or the blind, but also the paraplegic, attend classes in devalued public schools, how can they play, etc., when experts say that even books must be designed specifically for the blind and the deaf? in england, these measures were not even implemented, because they had provoked strong reactions in universities, disability associations, etc. the logic of integration is related to the reduction of social expenditure, the transfer of the cost to the family itself and the educational devaluation of the disabled. for all the reasons mentioned above, we as members of the european parliament from the communist party of greece will not vote in favour of the report.

the council. the council has repeatedly expressed its deep concern at the high tensions and widespread violence in southern serbia and the increasing number of incidents in the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, particularly near the border with the federal republic of yugoslavia incidents which the council strongly condemned in its conclusions of 26 february. at the same time, the support for efforts to achieve a peaceful solution to the conflict with full respect for the sovereignty and territorial integrity of the federal republic of yugoslavia. to this end, the union assessed at the general affairs council meeting on 19 march, following contacts with nato and the signing of a ceasefire agreement with the parties, that the safety of the observers was adequately in writing. - (el) contrary to what usually happens - the european parliament demanding more stringent measures than the council - in the case of directive 91/308/ec on the legalization of funds derived from illicit activities/money laundering, the european parliament cancels and in fact rejects the new measures that are proposed. parliament has the right to request that they be exempted from the obligation to observe professional secrecy in respect of new lawyers and their activities. however, this is not the case for members of the professions such as accountants, tax advisers, estate agents, notaries, auctioneers, etc., who are required to report cases to the courts where there is evidence of money laundering. therefore these categories should not be approved, and i voted against the rapporteur's amendments in question.
in writing. - (el) the food risks which have recently emerged, particularly in the economically developed countries of the eu, starting with bse due to the use of animal proteins for feeding livestock, were to lead to the permanent abolition of meat and bone meal and its replacement by vegetable proteins produced in the community so that the latter's agriculture would not depend on soybeans from the united states. in other words, a more general review of the cap was needed, both in the livestock sector and in the agriculture sector which produces animal feed. in our view, the quota system needed to be revised, particularly that of the mediterranean countries, and in particular my own, where quotas are far below levels of self-sufficiency and production capacity and where the mode of production of products of animal origin is incomparably healthier than the corresponding mode of production in the economically developed countries of the eu. the quotas they have are many times greater than their needs, so they export their food risks to all the deficit countries, like mine. in addition, the system of feed prices and aid for fodder plants had to be reviewed in the sense of support for fodder legumes, in order to ensure cheap and high-quality vegetable protein and to protect the environment, since legumes have a soil improving effect and are the best crops in land-use programmes, especially in mediterranean soil and climate conditions. instead, the committee on agriculture, using various tricks and believing that the bse problem would eventually be forgotten, worked, after six months, to reintroduce animal proteins into the livestock feed. in other words, it is trying to reintroduce meat and bone meal, which is now proven to be responsible for bse, with no regard for public health and in the interests of the community feed industries. these efforts are evidenced by the decisions taken so far on these issues and by the discussions taking place in official bodies. to divert attention from this attempt, the commission is using the proposal to develop the cultivation of fodder legumes on certain cereal farms which have been set aside, allegedly to increase the production of vegetable protein, but at the same time it is prohibiting the production of cereals on these farms, limiting it to straw, without even paying any aid. with this proposal, plant protein production will be minimal or even non-existent, even in countries where there are large areas of fallow land. but for our country, and perhaps for other mediterranean countries, the proposal is devoid of content, since the areas of fallow land are negligible or non-existent. with the commission's proposal, the problem of protein in the feed of the community livestock remains unresolved, with the consequence that its 'solution' is deliberately and consciously found either in the meat and bone meal which is responsible for bse or in genetically modified soya from the united states, which is perhaps just as dangerous for public health and which, moreover, makes community livestock dependent on the multinationals which market this plant and its derivatives. since the commission's proposal does not exploit the production capacities existing in the community countries to solve the problem of protein and, more generally, animal feed once and for all in a way that safeguards public health, and since it is intended to divert the attention of farmers and consumers in order to perpetuate the dangerous production of livestock products, and since i am not prepared to contribute, even indirectly, to the maintenance of this serious problem for public health and to the abuse of farmers and herders, i shall not vote in favour of the commission's proposal, believing that my vote will serve as a signal to the people of the community to fight for the production of healthy and safe food.

the rapporteur. - mr president, the stability and convergence programmes being assessed today by this house are the second update in a row of the original programmes submitted in 1999 for the first time by the 14 member states, with the exception of greece, which submitted its first programme this year. i would like to start with the last programme, which deals with situations which are both typical and, of course, very familiar to me. the programmes are part of the control and enforcement mechanism of the stability pact, a fiscal discipline pact whose two main objectives are to achieve a budget deficit of no more than 3% each year and also to achieve a balance or surplus over the period of a full economic cycle. these are the goals that, if you told greece in 1993 that its economy would have achieved them in just seven years, they would not have taken seriously. greece was the country known for inflation - 13% in 1993 - and deficits - over 14% of gdp in 1993. it was the example to be avoided within the european union. few believed he could overcome these situations. and yet, by the end of 2001, greece, as the council agrees, is expected to have a surplus of half a percentage point of gdp instead of a deficit, rising to 1.5% in 2002 and 2% in 2003. and instead of 13% inflation, it will have an inflation rate of just 2.3%. i have, with your permission, retained a few of these elements, mr president, because greece's accession to the culture of price stability and fiscal stability was perhaps the last step which sealed the success of this policy throughout europe. this spectacular turnaround of the last member state to meet the maastricht criteria is a general indication of the climate of monetary and fiscal stability that prevailed in europe as a result of the efforts of all to support the single currency. the economic committee in its resolution to you recognizes and welcomes the achievement of fiscal and price stability. it points out that the slight excess over the inflation target, as set by the central bank, is not due to internal economic weaknesses but to external shocks (oil price, euro depreciation against the dollar). it also stressed that budgetary equilibrium and hence monetary stability could be achieved in the medium term only on the basis of a strong european economy characterised by high output growth. the average growth rate reached 3.3% in 2000, with austria at 3.5%, finland at 5.2%, greece at 4.1%, ireland at an astonishing 10.7%, luxembourg at 8.3%, the netherlands at 4.5%, portugal at 3.3% - just above the european average -, spain at 4%, sweden at 3.9%. unfortunately, these rates of growth are not going to continue. higher oil prices, higher interest rates, and the slowdown in the us economy have led to a revision of the original forecasts to between 2.4% and 2.8%. so we cannot expect to have the same year of ease, the same budget surpluses as in 2000, without that being a deficit. the good organisation and use of public spending to support growth is therefore of particular importance, especially if we take into account the lisbon long-term objectives: restoring full employment within a decade, increasing the labour force participation rate to 70%, achieving an average growth rate of the economy of 3%, reorganising the welfare state and promoting the european economy at the forefront of the knowledge economy. the stability programmes submitted give evidence that member states are aware of these needs. they have directed the surpluses they have generated towards repayment of the public debt, tax cuts and increases in public investment and, in the longer term, the stability of the public pension system, which has also attracted much attention and some of its resources are channelled into a reserve fund. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has on this subject. in the present situation, characterized by the threat of a serious recession in the united states economy and the persistent need to boost the rate of economic growth in europe, debt repayment and tax relief seem less important to us than financing public investment, of course in balanced and non-deficit budgets, aimed both at supporting growth that is showing signs of exhaustion and at pushing the structural changes needed to transition to a modern, technologically advanced, full-employment economy in europe. it should be borne in mind that the absorption of high unemployment rates requires the steady achievement of a growth rate of 3%, a target which cannot be considered to have been achieved on a permanent basis in our economy. in conclusion, mr president, i would also in writing. - (el) in a period marked by major food scandals, with dangerous consequences for the health of consumers, even leading to deaths, one could have expected greater sensitivity to food safety issues. on the contrary, the report calls for a derogation from the ban on the use of veterinary medicinal products for which no maximum residue limit has been set in the feeding and fattening of meat-producing animals. we do not agree with this derogation, nor do we accept the argument of an alleged lack of economic interest of multinational pharmaceutical companies in the development of adequate veterinary medicines. on the contrary, we insist on the need to adopt and observe ever more stringent control procedures throughout the whole animal feeding cycle (feed, medicines, processing of food of animal origin, etc.) in order to control the production system of food of animal origin as completely as possible, and this, of course, without having any illusions about the possibility of obtaining, under a capitalist system, a control which fully guarantees the safety of the food produced. we also disagree with the rapporteur's call for the establishment of a pan-european system for the granting of marketing authorisations for medicinal products, which would in effect mean the abolition of the control systems and services of the member states, which are responsible for examining the dossiers of medicinal products to be placed on the market, since they would be obliged to accept the authorisation granted by another member state. for the reasons i have just mentioned, we as members of the communist party of greece will vote against the doyle report.

in writing. - (de) this report on the commission recommendation on the broad guidelines of the economic policies of the member states and the community in 2001 sets as a priority objective to make the european union the most competitive and dynamic economic area in the world by 2010. there is nothing to object to in principle, but the author omits - certainly not unconsciously - the fact that at the lisbon summit this phrase was completed as follows: an economic area coupled with a quantitative and qualitative improvement in employment and greater social cohesion. this is where the real problem lies. the economic downturn is rightly noted and, to counteract it, the neo-liberal methods are again used: economic policy based on supply, liberalization, reduction of public debt, the requirement of moderation of workers' demands in collective bargaining. although some important points missing from the report by the same author on the european economic situation have now been taken up - notably the need for certain harmonisation in tax law, the emphasis on the importance of strengthening smes and the consideration of environmental aspects - there are still some key elements missing. without a strengthening of domestic demand, i.e. an increase in purchasing power, it will not be possible to initiate a lasting economic recovery. that is why the preservation and creation of jobs must be given the highest priority. we urgently need a redistribution of employment through various forms of reduction in working time, a fair call for contributions from all those who earn income from work to social and pension funds, a stronger emphasis on education and training and a re-examination of the public authorities' investment policy. until these requirements are integrated into the european economic strategy, we cannot approve it.

commission. – the european commission of course strongly supports pressure from the international community to eradicate child trafficking and child forced labour, in particular in those countries of western and central africa where, regrettably, these practices are well-established in tradition and even tolerated by the national authorities. the european union has repeatedly condemned such policies and ensures that promoting children's rights is a priority in our relations with the countries concerned. the cotonou agreement reaffirms the parties' commitment to internationally-recognised core labour standards as defined by the international labour organisation, including child labour. as far as trade agreements are concerned, the european union seeks to raise support among trading partners for respective core labour standards, including child labour. the commission considers that additional tariff preferences under the scheme of generalised preferences should be considered for countries which effectively apply those standards. the commission also finances actions which directly address the issue. over eur 1.2 million was allocated to promote children's rights and to protect them from violence and exploitation in gabon, benin, togo and nigeria. the commission is also financing a project in benin that aims to strengthen a brigade for the protection of minors to foster more effective implementation of legislation and to raise public awareness on this issue. many other activities are undertaken by the relevant international organisations, in particular unicef and the international labour organisation. a recent positive signal is the likely ratification of the ilo convention on the prohibition of child trafficking. several west african countries, including cote d'ivoire, should have ratified it by the end of the month. the commission strongly supports these initiatives.

commission. - the commission communication on structural indicators, which presents the most recent data on social exclusion and poverty, shows that in 1995 in greece the indicators relating to the poverty rate, including social transfers, the persistent poverty rate and the ratio between the income of the population representing the top 20% of income and that representing the bottom 20% of income, were above the community average. this, of course, highlights a clear fact, namely that the situation in greece is worse than in the other countries of the european union. however, one very important fact should be clarified, and that is that we are referring only to data for 1995. where data on other characteristics or periods are available, this may be different. the attempt to link the problems of employment and poverty to a single factor is possibly wrong. in our view, tackling the problem of poverty must take account of several dimensions: the general economy, the structural reforms needed to improve the level of growth and the standard of living, and also the correct use of the structural funds and the implementation of specific policies to improve employment. in this sense, employment strategies can help to fight poverty. the same applies to social assistance policies or specific actions to deal with the specific problem of poverty, as was stressed at the nice european council. in this context, the greek government has undertaken to submit its national action plan against poverty and social exclusion by 1 july 2001, and that plan will be in force for a period of two years. the plan should include information on specific anti-poverty policy measures, including those recently announced by the greek government. it should also be remembered that in the field of the use of the structural funds, and particularly as regards the european social fund, there are also some specific measures to combat poverty and social exclusion, and in this respect some of the operational programmes for the greek case and also some of the regional programmes include measures on employment, education, health and welfare. all this should have a positive effect and change the situation to which i have referred and which is, of course, the one covered by the indicators for 1995.

the commission. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have to say that i am very pleased with the way the commission has been working. the commission is well aware of the enormous damage caused by the forest fires which occur every year in the forests of the european union and in particular in greece. in this context, i would like to inform you, mr president, that in the next few weeks the commission will approve, under regulation no 2158/92, a programme of protection against forest fires in greece, which provides for a financial volume of eur 1.7 million for 2001. now, to your other questions. firstly, the average annual area of reforestation in greece for the period 1994 to 1999 is 2 750 hectares. this corresponds to 15% of the area of forest burned in this member state. on the basis of the information available to the commission, a comparison can only be made with portugal, where approximately 5000 hectares were reforested annually during the same period; this is equivalent to 14% of the forest area burned. secondly, the forest area destroyed by fires each year in the member states is 73,500 hectares in spain, some 50,000 hectares in italy, 36,500 hectares in portugal and 19,000 hectares in greece. thirdly, it is very difficult for the commission to assess the efforts made in greece to restore the burned forest to its former state, because of course many factors are involved. the reafforestation of the areas concerned is, as you know, an integral part of the national forestry policy and the success of the restoration efforts depends in particular on several main factors, namely the annual potential of greece to carry out reforestation, the chance of natural forest rejuvenation on the fire-damaged areas and finally the afforestation of new areas, i.e. for example agricultural areas. in some cases, this will replace the forest area destroyed by the fire. finally, the success of the project depends, of course, on the quality of forest fire prevention and the efficiency of firefighting systems. this will reduce the amount of forest destroyed by fires in future years.

insurance funds, but mainly because of the lack of computerization system. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the commission is therefore not in a position to verify the accuracy of the data used by this company or the methodology used in carrying out this study. as regards the second question, which is the statistics on social security funds: greece is obliged to provide the commission with these statistics after 1994 and, of course, in the context of the procedures concerning the european union. there are difficulties in the flow of these data by the greek authorities in terms of their detailed collection and updating. since 1996, there has been some progress in this area, both because of the legalisation and rationalization of primary expenditure and better control of primary expenditure in the wider public sector, but it must be said that there are problems in the information received by the european union, largely due to the lack of detailed information, especially from certain funds. the third question concerns the modernisation of public administration, particularly the social security funds, through the kleisthenes programme. under the kleisthenes programme, there was a commitment to computerize the ika, which is the main social security institution, and to computerize the policy on the single social security number for all insured persons in greece. under the second community support framework and the kleisthenes framework, specific projects in these two areas had to be completed. the ika has started to reorganize and modernise, but there are delays, mainly due to legal disputes over the outcome of the call for tenders, unfortunately for the most important action plan, which is the integrated information system. in writing. - (el) the report, in its introduction, demonstrates, among other things, in a convincing and detailed way: a) that the probability of developing an addiction (alcoholism) in adults increases to a considerable extent if consumption has begun in childhood and adolescence; (b) that the consumption or abuse of alcohol by children and adolescents is increasing in all european countries; (c) that in many eu countries 'synthetic drinks' are produced, i.e. soft drinks containing alcohol which are sold uncontrolled to children and adolescents; (d) that there is a direct and alarming relationship between violent and antisocial behaviour and road traffic accidents (death, disability) and the consumption of alcohol by children and adolescents. the rapporteur is therefore right to raise these crucial questions. but in the face of this situation, where we would expect specific proposals to tackle this very serious problem, we are dealing with a series of amendments which either 'recommend', 'encourage' or 'advise' producers not to make 'synthetic woods', waiters and bar owners to avoid selling alcoholic beverages to children and adolescents and, instead, to put cheap non-alcoholic beverages on their cards! advertising should be directed at adults, novice drivers should avoid having a blood alcohol content of more than 0.2%, parents, sports associations and sportsmen should put pressure on children and teenagers to oppose the beatings of producers, retailers, bar and nightclub owners and advertisers. at the same time, we rely on their philanthropy to ensure that they do not leak and promote alcohol to children. in other words, these are incidental, trivial, fragmentary proposals which, at best, are naive since they are far from tackling a problem which is costing the health and lives of millions of miners. we have not seen any mention of the root causes of the problem, no proposals directed against the huge economic interests that produce, sell and promote alcohol among children and adolescents by any means, legal or illegal. it is clear that if these issues are not addressed, any proposal is a palliative, a wishful thinking and only serves to perpetuate and aggravate the problem. finally, it should be noted that the introduction stresses that these proposals, which are harmless to economic interests and speculation, should not be made binding and converted into legislation, but should remain recommendations for the member states.

in writing. - (el) while the treaty of nice, which determines the method of financing european political parties, has not yet been ratified by the member states, which would bring it into force, the council and the competent parliamentary committee have, with unnecessary haste, submitted a report on the status and financing of european political parties for a vote in the plenary. the pretext for this eagerness is allegedly the eu's concern about the increasing abstentionism in the elections. this argument has not the slightest shred of truth, in view of the large turnout recorded in the last elections in italy, but also in all the investigations on the subject, which speak of the disappointment and indifference of the voters (due to the inconsistency of the bourgeois parties, which do not match their actions with their speeches) with regard to what is happening in brussels or strasbourg. this distance will be further strengthened by the creation of european parties, which will have even looser ties with their voters. by using special financial incentives as bait, binding rules are in fact introduced for the creation and operation of european parties. these strict obligations concern, on the one hand, their status and, on the other hand, the electoral weight a party must have in order to be considered 'european' and to be entitled to the funding in question. thus, this party 'must clearly have european objectives, such as european integration, even if its approach seems opposed to it'. this "looks" is very suspicious. will a party which, like the communist party of greece, not only seems to have, but clearly "has" an opposite approach to european integration be accepted? furthermore, it is stated that a political party cannot be considered european if it declares itself to be "intransigent". in other words, is the declared intransigence to fascism, or more to the class enemy and the exploitation of the working class, a prohibition clause? as regards the electoral weight, it is proposed that the party must have members elected either to the european parliament or to the national parliaments of at least five member states (or in one third of the member states, or in one quarter of them, according to the text voted on today), or have obtained in at least five of the member states (or in one third, or in one quarter of the states) at least five per cent of the votes in the last european elections. this is a direct intervention in the electoral system of each country by introducing higher electoral thresholds, which in practice will exclude the votes of those who oppose the so-called 'european ideal', i.e. the imperialist nature of the eu. finally, in the name of transparency, it is planned to make the income public. but since there is no limit to the amount of income, it can be assumed that the names of party members and supporters who have paid their dues will also be recorded. the report considers the prohibition of donations to a party by public enterprises to be a matter of transparency. he 'd be willing to say otherwise . but the problem is the donations from the all-powerful private sector, which he's as silent as a carp. the compulsory creation of political parties which is being attempted to be introduced constitutes a gross intervention in the political system of each country. it aims to weaken the role of national parties, especially those opposed to the eu. the parties are institutions which should express the free will of their members, both in terms of their programme and their rules of operation. the creation of european political parties, imposed from above, is intended to make the peoples accept the one-way imperialist integration of the eu as a fatal and obligatory process, to strike a blow at dissenting voices - and, if possible, to exclude them from the european parliament - but also to further distance the workers from the decision-making centres that directly concern them.
in writing. - (el) the amendments proposed by the rapporteur from the committee on agriculture represent an improvement on the commission's proposal, but they also fall short of the logic of the of the olive oil market, set prices at degrading levels and squandered the income of olive growers, who are all small and medium-sized farmers from arid and island regions. the commission is consciously and deliberately ignoring these consequences and is not in any way considering reintroducing intervention, which would ensure a minimum income for olive growers. it does not propose any increase in quotas, which would at least cover existing production, but it is supposedly focusing its attention on the quality of the oil, believing that the product which has hitherto been the healthiest and best suited to the health of consumers was completely unsuitable. the commission's pseudo-interest in the quality of olive oil is hypocritical and is intended to divert attention. while adulterated in packages of less than five litres. in other words, the measure is not intended to improve quality but to allow industrialists and multinationals to monopolize the marketing and secondary importance. unfortunately, the commission is covering up its unacceptable actions with the complicity of the greek government.

i voted against the second part of paragraph 23 of the joint motion for a resolution on the gothenburg european council for the following reasons: firstly, because there is no official commission proposal to phase out tobacco subsidies, only a reference by the president of the european commission, mr romano prodi, to the debate on sustainable development. the wording of paragraph 23 is therefore inaccurate, secondly, the idea of abolishing tobacco subsidies to combat smoking is hypocritical. it is hypocritical because such measures, in a european union with a tobacco deficit, achieve two objectives: on the one hand, to punish tobacco producers in the european south, and on the other hand, to support tobacco producers in other third countries from which tobacco will now be imported in order to cover the resulting tobacco deficit, which will also be a burden on the european union's trade balance. thirdly, because there are areas of europe, such as the province of xanthi in northern greece, where the rare basmas variety of tobacco has been grown for generations, and where over 20 000 farmers are exclusively engaged in the cultivation of mountainous, inaccessible, very small areas of 30 to 300 square metres where nothing else can be grown, and which would be completely impoverished if the ideas put forward were to be implemented.

in writing. - (el) the pasok group of meps voted against the second part of article 23 of the resolution for the gothenburg european council, because it considers that the abolition of tobacco cultivation in europe, which is the aim of this provision, will have absolutely no effect on the total quantities of tobacco used by the european tobacco industry, since the european share of demand (about 30% of the total) will immediately be replaced by imports from countries outside the european union, to the detriment of european producers. the pasok meps believe that the fight against the pathogenic effects of smoking is not helped by ineffective measures which are a vain gesture and only satisfy the need for hypocrisy of those who have focused the health concerns of european citizens on a single point of doubtful effectiveness.
the commission is aware of the fact that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. as you know, this directive has a special meaning for me. the commission submitted its first proposal at the end of 1998 to introduce common rules applicable to all professional drivers, and since then this directive has been a complicated and tortuous process, which has been blocked in the council for all these years. in 2000, an amendment to the general directive on the organisation of certain aspects of working time, directive 93/104/ec, was adopted to include mobile and non-mobile workers in the transport sector. however, despite this amendment, mobile workers are covered only in respect of a number of matters, in particular the average working week of 48 hours, annual leave, health checks for night workers and the guarantee of sufficient rest time. it is therefore still entirely appropriate to maintain and defend the proposal for a directive which we are now discussing. after having seen how difficult it was to make progress in the council and with the concern to make a step forward, even if it was not the best, i presented at the end of 2000 an amended proposal for a directive which, as you know, provided for the automatic inclusion of selfemployed drivers in the long term, namely after three years. i fully agree with what has been said here, because the directorate-general for energy and transport is responsible for regulating the working time of drivers beyond driving time, for safety reasons. because a driver's fatigue does not depend on whether he is self-employed or employed. i have no doubt that that is my position. i therefore proposed to the council remind you that the concepts of working time and driving time are two different concepts, regulated by two different legal instruments. and in that sense, there is an amendment, amendment no 23, which characteristic that, according to the proposal, the director-general submits the work programme to the governing council after approval by the commission and then the governing council adopts the programme. the director-general is appointed and dismissed by the management board, but only on a proposal from the commission. we are therefore witnessing a preliminary mistrust of the european commission towards the body it proposes to set up, which is manifested in a tendency to patronize it and to limit its guarantees of independence, which is not seen in the case of the corresponding community bodies. a comparison with the status of other bodies, such as the european environment agency or the european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products, leads to the conclusion that these bodies have a wider scope of initiatives and responsibilities. for example, there is no provision for the commission to approve the work programme of the agency for the evaluation of medicinal products, whereas similar arrangements apply to the environment agency. nor is there any provision for the commission to be responsible in the event of the director-general of these bodies being draft opinion of the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the modern way of life is so dependent on oil as an energy source that if it were to suddenly cease to exist, it would paralyse almost everything. can you, for example, imagine our lives without air transportation and without cars? the commission's communication, which is extremely important in its content and bold in its findings on the oil supply to europe, mr linkohr's report on it, the opinions of the committees on transport, the environment and economic and monetary policy, which i have the honour to be asked to deliver an opinion on, are clear and, to a large extent, all converge on a few key findings. first, world oil consumption will rise from 77 million barrels a day in 2000 to 115 million barrels a day in 20 years, at current rates, almost doubling. secondly, experts predict that peak daily production will not be able to exceed 90 million barrels and that new discoveries are now on a declining trend. thirdly, europe's dependence on oil imports is absolute. it is currently 75% and is forecast to exceed 85% by the year 2020. fourth, the price of crude oil is forecast to remain high, at least between usd 22 and usd 28 per barrel, with a more likely tendency to rise further. if this is combined with the high dollar price, then the increase in costs and therefore the lower competitiveness of european products, which will also be due to the high taxation of petroleum products in the european union, is inevitable. of course, this will also increase inflationary pressures. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's transport policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new technologies. sixthly, the pollution of the air on our planet, note this, is due to 70% of transport and only 7% to industry, and let us confine our criticism misleadingly to the polluting industry alone, whereas cars and airplanes are the main polluters of our planet. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but oil is valuable for other, more important uses, such as the production of medicines. therefore, as it is also a non-renewable resource, it should not be wasted. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council will be able to accept it. in the long term, we, and the european union in particular, must turn to other sources of energy, such as natural gas, the hydrogen that is abundant on our planet, nuclear energy - with emphasis on fusion - and of course all the other sustainable sources, among which solar energy has a prominent place. in the short term, mr president, the european union should look for oil and gas from more geographical sources, particularly in the caucasus region. it must also seek an agreement with opec on more stable prices and payment in euro instead of dollars, while increasing its safety reserves. madam president, at least for reasons of equality, it was necessary to create a right analogous to copyright or other intellectual property rights in intellectual works with unlimited reproduction. and that is the right to observe the work in the field of visual arts. the nature of copyright is proprietary, because it confers economic power and benefit on the creator. however, it is a special property right, because it recognizes the priority of aesthetic values and at the same time it pays a kind of tribute to the creators of works of art. it is a fact that cultural products cannot circulate without the contribution of art dealers. however, unlike the marketing of consumer or other capital goods, their marketing is not an end in itself. the marketing of works of art is concerned primarily with the dissemination of aesthetic values and messages. but this cannot be done without the participation of the creators. but while their contribution is irreplaceable, the market value of the products produced is gradually created. it is therefore totally unjustified for the merchant who is merely an intermediary to be the sole owner of this value. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to support the development of the audiovisual sector. for all these reasons it is wrong for the creators of certain countries not to recognize this right. we must therefore redress the error of the berne convention which made the right of surveillance possible. the obsession of some countries with long transitional periods or postponement of implementation is not flattering to its supporters. the european union, as the foremost centre of creativity and culture, must also show the way to third countries. no one will be harmed by this solution.

the commission's communication of december last on the community objectives for the 33rd icao assembly is the reference framework for preparing and evaluating the results to be achieved at the 33rd icao assembly, both in terms of reducing noise and noise emissions from aircraft and in terms of reducing gas emissions. i would like to say that the transport council last april was right to support this strategy proposed by the commission. this communication is very explicit in terms of the achievement of agreements at the icao level, both in terms of reducing aircraft noise and measures to reduce greenhouse gases, and in terms of limiting the operations of aircraft which only marginally meet the noise standards of chapter 3, and which are the major contributors to noise at airports. the conclusion of an agreement on the occasion of this assembly, with a resolution that will enable the contracting countries to meet their obligations in terms of reducing greenhouse gases in an easier or more comfortable way, is also part of our objectives. ladies and gentlemen, there is one aspect which we can already say is likely to have a good outcome at the icao assembly. i am, of course, talking about an agreement on the constitution of a new chapter 4, with a reduction of 10 decibels compared with the maximum emissions authorized in the previous chapters. in this connection, the icao council has also adopted rules to ensure that aircraft recertification is strictly enforced and thus to avoid the current situation with recertified or reclassified aircraft, which have moved from chapter 2 to chapter 3 and which is at the root of our whole dispute with the united states. in addition, the preparations by the icao assembly are intended to ensure that contracting countries have the flexibility to put in place those instruments which allow - in the language of icao - a balanced response to noise management. this balanced management is based on four elements: first, measures to reduce noise at source. secondly, rules on the occupation of land. thirdly, so-called noise-reducing procedures, which allow pilots to limit noise during take-off and landing. and finally, measures to limit the operation of chapter 3 aircraft which are noisier. it is above all this last point which, as you know, raises the greatest difficulties in our discussions with the united states. to facilitate agreement on this point at icao level, we are pursuing parallel discussions with the united states and developing countries. it should be noted that neither icao nor, of course, the united states can ignore the problem that aircraft noise poses for many airports in densely populated regions. in this context, we note that at some large airports this type of noise disturbance is reaching really critical limits and that it is therefore absolutely urgent to introduce measures to remedy this situation and to allow these airports to continue to operate. for others, however, the noise problem is far less important. we should therefore consider decisions - we are working on this - where there is a certain margin of appreciation for each airport. this is also an element which will make it easier for us to reach an agreement with the united states. however, there is a risk that different approaches to limiting the operation of chapter 3 aircraft - which are noisier - may lead to distortions of competition between different airports. in this connection, and in order to avoid an explosion of the community situation, it is intended to negotiate with the united states and, of course, in the icao framework, that this individual decision of each airport may be framed within a general framework which establishes different levels of operational restrictions according to special sensitivities. in any event, i once again pledge myself, together with the commission services, to maintain an open dialogue with you during all these months and i hope that we will eventually reach an agreement at the icao level which will enable us to ensure that this issue of the noisiest airports and aircraft is resolved and at the same time avoid any kind of confrontation with the united states, which would not benefit anyone.

mr president, mr president of the european central bank, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we are discussing today the excellent report by mrs randzio-plath on the annual report of the european central bank. it is the only way we can discuss the policy of the most important and most independent european institution. mr president, this debate is taking place in the context of radically changed global economic conditions. the negative economic environment, which has brought us to the point of being almost in recession, has created a lot of pressure. there is a lot of pressure on the european central bank and other central banks around the world to ease monetary policy. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are protected, and that the community's financial interests are protected. i think that leads us to a clear message. the central banks cannot be driven by the short-term horizon of the money markets, nor by their enthusiasm or pessimism. mr president, you must be guided by the principles of monetary stability and economic growth as enshrined in the treaty. but to gain greater confidence in the euro, the people of europe and the world's financial markets must gain greater confidence in the way you conduct your policy and in the way you achieve monetary stability. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
commission. – mr president, there are two main issues here. first, the transparency of the pension systems. one of the objectives of the third axis in the modernisation of pension systems is to make them transparent. this means that information must be clear and readily available not only to eu citizens but also to third-country nationals. there are major problems in this area at present. in order to achieve the degree of transparency aimed at, we need some kind of indicators. clear information should be available on demand. we cannot impose the reform structure or the structure of pension systems on member states, but we can measure it with a series of indicators. unfortunately, i cannot give you any more details on this at the moment. second, the issue of lifelong learning and the problem of early retirement. there is a clear link between the age limit and the need of active ageing. the majority of member states have an age limit of 65, but this is not the rule, it is the exception. there are many different early retirement systems in place in all member states which have caused many problems regarding the financial sustainability of the pension systems. that is why we propose this combination of policies: on the one hand, we propose the reform of the pension system and a change in the age limit, on the other hand, we propose policies for activating ageing populations, i.e. incentives for retaining older people in the labour market, incentives for training and, in some cases, subsidies for establishing an sme or other undertaking. so i very much agree with you, we need this integrated approach. we cannot just decide to increase the age limit, we must implement other policies at the same time.
mr president, first of all i must congratulate the commissioner, and i think that the congratulations are not only mine, but seem to be from the vast majority. even mr fatuzzo, who represents the pensioners, is very pleased. and indeed, the ones mentioned are impressive. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken the necessary steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. the question is, when article 140 speaks of a high level of protection, how can we mean reform without maintaining this high level of protection. but above all, i would like to ask two questions: are we talking about decent pensions through the complementarity of the pillars? we agree. have you considered whether this complementarity can be applied across the whole of the employee pyramid, and in particular to smes and low-income workers, or should there be a different approach in these cases? secondly, since the objective is sustainable pensions, and indeed decent pensions, in order to achieve this objective, should we also look at the scope for increasing the gross proceeds to be allocated to pensions?
