mr. president, war is not the answer. let's not turn our grief for the victims into a war cry. these were the main slogans of thousands of americans who demonstrated last saturday in the streets of washington. on our side of the atlantic, many tens of thousands of demonstrators in athens, rome, berlin, paris, amsterdam, prague, barcelona, stockholm, istanbul and elsewhere have raised their voices against the warmongering plans of the american and european imperialists. the european union must not forget that the united states is the only country in the world which has not yet been attacked by terrorists, and that the united states is the only country in the world which has not yet been attacked by terrorists. at the same time, within the countries of the european union and the united states, police repression is accelerating to silence those who resist or question their anti-people policies. the president of the united states has said that the war on terror will be a long one. the same will be true of the resistance of the popular forces for peace. we are confident that the law of peace will prevail against the barbarity of imperialist war.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are joining forces with the united states in a global campaign to root out terrorism. we cannot here offer much wisdom as to the technical, police or military measures that will have to be taken. what is in our area is to speak with perhaps more authority about certain political conditions for success. no campaign of this kind, no war, at least no war in which democracies have participated, has been able to create the necessary alliance of peoples - for that is the crucial alliance, not so much the alliance of governments - unless it offers the vision of a better world that will emerge at the end of that campaign. politically we must re-emphasise our support for all democratic regimes, and above all the rejection of tyrannical regimes which may today promise cooperation against specific terrorist organisations, but in the long term work together with the secret services which work with them as recruiters of terrorists. in the social field we must recognize that environmental destruction, pandemic diseases, poverty and misery are threats to the security of the world and must be treated as such, as terrorism. we must declare that in a globalized society security for the average man can be found neither in our wealth nor in our military might. the only realistic solution is to look for ways to reduce tensions in social and national struggles, in the exchange of all people of good will, regardless of race, religion, social status, ideology, all those who are determined to put an end to the scourges of humanity, which although they do not lead to terrorism, they cultivate the ground for terrorism, and this ground must be removed to ensure the safety of the citizen.

in writing. - (el) the disaster in toulouse, for which i too express my deep regret, must be an incentive for us to review the community directive on high-risk sites and to apply it more rigorously. we know that the revised seveso directive is not applied by many eu member states, whereas it should have been since 1999. worse still, it turns out that the original seveso directive is not being applied satisfactorily in many countries. i particularly welcome the fact that in our joint resolution we ask the commission to publish within three months a list of all high-risk industrial sites in the eu which are located in populated areas and which are likely, in the event of an accident, to cause damage on a scale similar to that of the toulouse disaster. petrola hellas saci has such a site near the highly populated areas of eleusis, aspropyrgos and mandra and has already applied for a permit to extend them over an area of 13 hectares in the non-buildable security zone between its current location and the pyrgal armament factory. i would also like to point out that, according to the testimony of workers at the plant in question, the operator did not make available to the public, as required by the seveso directive, either the safety report or the emergency plans for taking the necessary measures in the event of an accident. similar practices (defective plans, lack of information to local authorities and citizens) are observed at other industrial sites in the region. usually, an incomplete file is simply sent to the ministry without any subsequent application of the provisions of the seveso directive. the attention and concern of the competent greek authorities to prevent and limit the consequences of serious accidents is well illustrated by the response of the commissioner for the environment to a request i made. i quote: 'the commission sent a letter to the greek authorities on 7 june 2000, asking to be informed about the extension of the facilities of the company petrola. to date, the greek authorities have not replied to the letter in question'.
in writing. - (el) the two reports, one on asylum procedures for refugees and the other on eu immigration policy, are in fact intended to deal with the same problem. the problem of the mass influx into eu countries of people who are in danger in their own countries because of political persecution or economic survival. in the reports, as in other relevant eu documents, there is always a paragraph that refers to the need to take action to tackle the root causes of expatriation or immigration. what is not mentioned, of course, is the criminal responsibility of imperialism, which for centuries has exploited the natural and human resources of the third world countries and does not hesitate to make and unmake generally despotic governments in order to better serve its economic interests. the bitter struggle of the imperialist forces to overthrow socialism, the war they have ignited in the balkans and the latest tragic example of the war they are waging against their own taliban and against bin laden, have had and will continue to have as a consequence the wandering of millions of unhappy people in the eu countries and elsewhere, in search of a place in the sun. therefore, let us have no illusions, the refugee and migrant situation will have its day as long as imperialism reigns. so there is a need to manage the problem better. in this context, the evans report on asylum contains positive indications and proposals. but there is still a catalogue of pious wishes. for example, he rightly argues that the concept of 'safe' countries of origin or 'safe' third countries is very variable and carries with it the risk that people will be sent back to countries where they are in danger, in other words that we are literally sending them back to the 'mouth of the wolf'. turkey is a significant case, an accession country where, according to the eu, there is a steady improvement in the field of human rights. in reality, as we know, people are imprisoned for their ideas, hunger strikers and other democracy activists are murdered, parties that represent millions of kurds or that fight the regime (such as the communist party) are banned, trade unionists are arrested, etc. yet there are eu countries, including greece, which are turning back to this country ... "safe" opponents of the regime. as far as the living conditions of refugees are concerned, the case of greece is typical: thousands of kurds are in ghetto camps in lavrion, afghans and other refugees sleep in cardboard boxes in public gardens in athens, totally destitute. the report also rightly highlights the considerable slowness of the asylum procedure and the reluctance of states to grant asylum. in greece, for example, the average approval rate is 5% per year, and only this year it reached 7%. we shall vote for the evans report because it contains proposals to improve the current protection status of refugees. we believe that only the solidarity and common struggle of refugees and workers in eu countries to demand decent living conditions for all and even, beyond that, to eliminate the causes of expatriation and poverty, will bring a definitive solution to the problems.
in writing. - (el) the establishment of rules on migration flows to the european union, the assessment of the needs of the countries of entry, the procedures for the integration of third-country nationals into european union societies are in any case positive initiatives. but in the commission's communication on 'immigration policy', there are gaps, and other objectives remain to be promoted, in particular: - a more explicit definition of the terms 'migrant worker' and 'economic immigrant', avoiding the risk of creating new discrimination, based on the strict interests of the labour market in european countries; - family reunification must be accepted as a general rule and not as an attribute specific to certain categories of migrants; - increased mobility within the eu for all immigrants with a legal residence permit; - the fight against trafficking in human beings; - a strategy to encourage the return of immigrants with qualifications in order to strengthen the productive capacity of the countries of origin; - the elimination of discrimination against immigrants on grounds of sex, age, sexual orientation or disability; - the creation of a european migration network as an observatory and census mechanism. in this context, my vote was against.
in writing. - (el) the terrorist attacks in the united states challenge us to look at the problem of arms exports from a new angle. the titley report, for example, although it contains some positive recommendations, is in general terms, in my view, in no way responsive to the needs of the time. it is not acceptable for the european union to set the 'needs of european industry' as the criterion for its code of conduct on arms exports, since this would render any essential measure that could be mentioned obsolete. the european union will have to act in such a way that the united states, russia, china and all the other major arms exporting countries adopt binding rules of conduct - but their lack of readiness cannot be a factor of relaxation or an excuse for a failure to intervene on the part of the union. at the same time, there must be an eu-wide monitoring, recording and transparency of arms exports, in order to put an end to the scandalous practice of selling arms to forces that resort to crime and destabilisation and do not hesitate, through terrorism, to turn weapons against the european union itself and its citizens. for the reasons stated above, i consider the resolution to be insufficient and i did not vote for it.
mr president, mr president-in-office, mr commissioner, my view is that our economic policy is a combination of success and failure. the success is certainly the euro, the failure is the real economy. there are two problems and they are interrelated. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat this problem. the reason for both is common and lies in the low rate of fixed capital investment in the last decade of the last century, accompanied by the collapse of public investment. some argue that this collapse is virtual because it's due to a simple name change, because the private sector is investing where the public sector used to invest. but how can we believe that when the total investment has fallen so low? was it necessary to strangle public investment to prop up the euro? the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the euro is not used as a means of financing the single market, and that the single market is not used as a means of financing the single market. i do not believe it, but that was the official policy, without admitting it, until the lisbon european council. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. nice words, nice ambitions, and a nice vision. it has failed, unfortunately, because it coincided with the sudden deflation of the american stock market, especially in the areas of new technology and telecommunications, which were supposed to lead us into a new era, and now threaten to drag us down the precipice of one of the greatest recessions of the twentieth century. it seems to me that these events put an end to the lisbon vision and that we should think again. it is not possible to have a complete plan to present to you, especially when all the think tanks we have are denying the need to change economic policy. but some things seem obvious. the european economy needs investment, the private sector is not investing enough, why do we insist on preventing the public sector from supplementing it and perhaps thereby reviving the interest of the private sector? why do we refuse to use the possibilities offered by the treaty to exclude investment from the definition of the deficit in the stability pact? i have heard my colleague, mr della vedova, refer to 'deficit spending' with fear. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i am sure that the council will be able to accept them, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. america approached it in the last five years of the last century in a climate of excessive speculation, which is what is leading us today. i would like to ask you, commissioner, as you do me the honour to allow me to ask you privately: what is the scientific criterion for the refutability of your position that will make you tell us that our position has been refuted and that we must change economic policy? because as you should know, without refutability there is no scientific standing. we are constantly falling into a huge identity and i very much fear that this is exactly the economic policy you are advocating.
mr president, to reassure the commissioner and the other members, i am not a supporter, and i say this openly, of deficits, of increasing public debt or of inflation. however, that is not the question we are discussing. yes or no. are we predicting a recession? some say we should wait and see if he comes. if he comes, it'll be too late. others tell us to expect automatic solutions. some have the hidden hope that we will get a recovery from the recovery in the united states, completely ignoring of course that in a globalized world, if that happens, others will benefit from this recovery. the whole argument, mr president, is, as some colleagues have said before, to avoid the notion of public intervention in the economy. and i don't understand why. what kind of dogmatism is that when you have concrete problems, and even recessional ones, in front of you? but can anyone imagine, mr president, with various scenarios, both public intervention and stability in the economy, and increased revenue, without creating this dreadful tax competition that some of us are projecting? mr president, there is a problem of the need for public expenditure, there is a potential for public revenue which does not affect the production structure and the development of the economy or its competitiveness. don't tell me you'd have a problem with that if somewhere someone touched the economics of the pensioners, the international speculators of the hot money of the stock markets or even-even the excessive wealth and the extravagant consumption of the exaggerated upper middle - uppermiddle to put it better - strata in our societies. there is no scientific answer to that. there is a dogma. i am not saying that we should not be able to do this, but we should be able to do it in a way that is not too difficult for the citizens of europe. hey, i've never heard of such dogmatism!
mr president, by some diabolical coincidence, airplanes are the very spectacle that makes us understand the world today. it's the tragedy of new york, but it's also what happened yesterday in another dimension, on another level, at the airports in switzerland. and it could have been swissair, but it could have been sabena or olympic or some other airline instead of swissair. it is not the case that the european union is not a political union, but that it is a political union. it is our right. but to have a dogma that makes us not see what even the ultraconservative in its economic philosophy government of mr bush or mr o'nill in the united states sees, i think is an exaggeration. we should not look at the 15 billion given to airlines, we should not see that, for political and economic reasons, international capital flows must be controlled for economic and political stability, we should not see that governments should no longer be small, but take matters into their own hands. in this sense, i think that to stick dogmatically to a stability pact decided a decade ago and in other circumstances is a big mistake.

counsel. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the questions raised by the honourable member concern the naturalisation of citizens in a member state of the union and their repatriation. the report mentions suspicions of practices which are currently the subject of an investigation requested from the public prosecutor's office. finally, the question also raises an alleged link with the schengen agreements. i should like to remind you that the council has no jurisdiction to comment on the circumstances in which member states are obliged to grant or not to grant nationality to their own country. in fact, the policy on naturalisation is a matter for the member states. the application of these provisions is therefore, in the present case, the responsibility of the greek government, to which the honourable member should refer himself directly for answers to the questions he has raised. finally, it seems to the council that the honourable member could usefully refer to the commission's reply to question e-1438/01 in july for the years prior to the one here mentioned, and ask the commission to provide the figures for 1999 and 2000, as soon as these figures are available.

counsel. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the questions raised by the honourable members concern the conditions for the reintroduction of internal border controls in a state applying the schengen acquis and the conditions for reporting to the schengen information system in connection with the events which took place in genoa during the g8. as regards the first point, this situation does not correspond to what the honourable members called in their question, and i quote, 'the abolition of the schengen convention'. the reintroduction of controls is a measure explicitly provided for in article 2 (2) of the convention implementing the schengen agreement. and this article, as well as the procedure for applying this article, adopted by the political authorities of the states applying the schengen acquis in 1995, are part of the schengen acquis, incorporated into european union law. the italian authorities considered that public policy or national security required that national border controls, adapted to the situation, be carried out at internal borders during the g8 summit in genoa, in particular from 14 to 21 july 2001. this is an exceptional measure, but one which other states have already used in the past at political or sporting events which could have created risks to public order. as regards the second point, i would remind the honourable members of the answer we gave at the june 2001 part-session on the same subject, namely that the council is not competent to answer the question whether certain persons involved in these events have been reported to the schengen information system. the entry of an alert into sis is a matter for the member states. it is recalled that article 94 of the convention implementing the schengen agreement states that 'the schengen information system shall contain only those categories of data which are supplied by each of the contracting parties and which are necessary for the purposes laid down in articles 95 to 100. the reporting contracting party shall verify whether the importance of the case justifies the inclusion of the alert in the schengen information system. in addition, article 104 states that 'the national law of the contracting party issuing the alert shall apply to the alert, except where a more stringent condition is laid down in this convention'. finally, i would remind the honourable member that the schengen acquis was published in official journal l 239, dated 22 september 2000.

counsel. - look, i can only confirm what i said. if there was a translation error, i apologize. you put things back the way they were. as for the rest, i have noted your comments and will pass them on.
council. – the only thing i can confirm is that it is the responsibility of the member states to enter personal data into the schengen information system. under the convention they are obliged to see to it that the importance of the situation justifies any such input into the system. this is, then, the responsibility of the member states.

council. - the council would point out to the honourable member that the judgments he mentions were given by the european court of human rights in strasbourg. the court was thus set up by the council of europe and not by the court of justice of the european communities in luxembourg. the european union does not currently have any concrete evidence to suggest that turkey will not comply with the rulings of the european court of human rights in this case. more generally, the european union does not have a list of rulings of the european court of human rights that turkey has not complied with. the council of the european union recalls that it has no jurisdiction to enforce the decisions of the european court of human rights. i would like to stress on behalf of the council that the union, particularly in the context of the enhanced political dialogue with turkey, will take every opportunity to stress the obligation of that country to respect all the international commitments it has entered into. the european parliament has been a very active participant in the process of democratization in the european union. in the context of the accession partnership with turkey, the european union has stipulated that turkey must respect and guarantee human rights and fundamental freedoms and ensure that they are also put into practice. the council will study turkey's progress in this area with care after the commission has submitted its report on the matter. so i can only repeat once again that the council does not have a list of european court of human rights rulings which turkey has allegedly ignored.

in writing. - (el) the introduction of the 'television without frontiers' directive was an important step in community legislation. but more than ten years have passed without the editorial adjustments required to protect the spectator as well as european artistic production. three points are of particular importance: (a) the protection of viewers against advertising, by making the provisions of the directive more binding, with control and penalties for channels which infringe them; (b) the generalization at community level of the protection of minors against advertising and programmes which encourage violence and antisocial behaviour; (c) the adoption of more stringent, concrete measures to promote european production and measures to facilitate more the penetration of producers from small countries into the european market as a whole.
in writing. - (el) the single currency requires a single economic governance. this is the main conclusion reached in the commission's report and in the report of the rapporteur. the exercise of economic policy by member states is even more restricted in a context where decisions are taken in advance, and national parliaments are transformed into mere observers or, worse, into executors of eu decisions. this is an attempt to weaken the reactive forces of workers who are increasingly resolutely opposed to the choices of big business. the ep report endorses the eu's anti-social policy and puts forward proposals which can only serve as an alibi for the eu's ultra-neo-liberal economic and social policy and its tragic consequences for the working classes, such as the prior agreement on the allocation of fortuitous or exceptional tax revenues, for fear that amounts will be spent on the social security sector. the integration of the problem of the consequences of population ageing into national budgets is moving in the same direction. while the report itself states that unemployment continues to affect 14 million europeans and that the socioeconomic situation has not improved, it remains faithful to the policy of restructuring and sharing unemployment. the participation of national parliaments in the annual review of the implementation of the broad economic policy guidelines adopted by the european council and the discussion of the stability programmes approved by the commission and the council is only a semblance of democracy designed to lock national representations into community procedures, all the more so when it is proposed to strengthen the role of the commission and to impose sanctions on member states which do not apply the economic policy imposed by brussels. the aim is to make the competent national institutions complicit in a process which, in the name of coordinating economic policies within the framework of the broad guidelines, subjects wage policy measures and social policy more generally to the stifling constraints of the convergence criteria and the stability pact. the member states are expressly invited 'to coordinate their actions and to refrain from adopting measures of a fiscal, budgetary or wage policy nature'. the dismantling of the guaranteed social security system, together with the reduction of wages, the regulation of labour time and cost in the interests of employers, are now leading to a worsening of poverty and social exclusion among workers and a decline in the quality and volume of benefits offered. it is clear that the more measures are adopted to reduce income and workers' rights, the more pressures are mounted for further structural reforms, and the more signs of recession become clear, the more austerity policies will be tightened.

exploitation of resources, markets and consumers. as the report notes, the number of mergers peaked in 2000 with 345 new cases, thanks to increasingly flexible legislation that favours the creation of as you know, mr president, the schengen information system has been in operation since 1995, but it has been a community instrument since 1999, as a result of the integration of the schengen acquis into the framework of the european union by the treaty of amsterdam. its community identity therefore implies that any proposed replacement should be bound by the same legal provisions governing its establishment and operation, and that any extension of its purpose should be subject to full democratic control by the competent institutions, as previous colleagues have pointed out. the current state of the art, with the need to take additional measures to protect citizens, and the corresponding weakness of the current information system should not be a potential area for arbitrary derogations and extensions of its powers. the proposed legal framework is necessary to clarify some critical issues. in order to ensure the protection of natural persons with regard to the processing of personal data, the assistance of the joint supervisory authority established by the schengen convention is required. the authority has been given the task of drawing up an annual report on the operation of the existing system, which should now include a systematic review and analysis of the new data generated by the development of the new system. at the same time, the european parliament should step up its efforts to monitor effectively the new features of the system, requiring us to be regularly informed of progress. it should be noted that the council has only informed the european parliament once about the further implementation of the schengen convention, with the result that the european parliament has not been informed of issues directly related to the free and unhindered movement of citizens and the way in which the union's borders are controlled. finally, reference should be made to the need to establish a community information system, consisting of a single electronic network, to facilitate the transparent storage, management and evaluation of data.
mr president, the schengen information system, which has been in operation since 1995, has served and continues to serve with europol as a tool to facilitate european repression, the construction and tracking of suspects, the combating of resistance movements and civil disobedience and the eradication of the migrant invader who arrives in europe seeking a place in the sun. it was used with particular zeal whenever popular resistance was organized in the interests of the european union, in nice, gothenburg, genoa. the european parliament debated a progress report a month ago in which the rapporteur gave some very revealing evidence of the arbitrariness and unreliability of the personal data recorded. a few days later, the european union hastened to put these elements at the disposal of the americans to facilitate their task of stifling every voice of resistance anywhere and unleashing a new imperialist war under the pretext of fighting terrorism. and as if that were not enough, at the recent extraordinary european council, the 15 members of the european union granted the united states unfettered access to all european sources and archives. the schengen system, whether it is the first generation or the second generation we are discussing today, is a component of the repressive mechanism and its abolition is therefore the subject of a popular movement's struggle.

the amendment of the 1976 directive is a very important development, mr president, and it shows the commission's concern for gender equality. the committee on women's rights, for its part, thanks to the tireless efforts of our rapporteur, mrs hautala, and of other colleagues, has contributed to the presentation today of an improved text which is very widely accepted. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i hope that the council will take them up. the new directive certainly responds to the new conditions created over the last twenty-five years in the field of employment and vocational training for women and replaces an outdated legal framework which has contributed most to discrimination against women, to serious deviations from the principle of equality, to high unemployment rates, to the exclusion of women from many occupational fields and, more generally, to the division of the labour market by sex. i would like to inform you that the greek parliament recently replaced by a near-unanimous vote a similar provision of the constitution which also allowed for deviations from the principle of gender equality in the labour market. the hautala report takes a step towards a better framework and introduces new areas of action, such as measures to prevent and combat sexual harassment at the workplace, effective protection of workers from unequal treatment linked to pregnancy and motherhood, judicial protection, positive measures to put equality into practice, as many colleagues have pointed out, and sanctions in cases of infringement of the directive. however, i think that the directive could be clearer in setting objective criteria for selection for any job and leave no room for exceptions and exclusions based on sex.

mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the question of the nomenclature of territorial units is certainly not a formal one, as it might seem at first sight, it is not a technical discussion in statistical terms, but a substantive one, since the adoption of any classification has direct implications for supporting the development of each territorial unit. in view of the enlargement, or rather the enlargements, and since the candidate countries will be admitted in groups, it is necessary to make progress towards the objective classification of territorial units, so that the new members know the rules of the game. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the criteria for classification are uniform for all member states. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the legal basis for the nomenclature of statistical territorial units is therefore to be welcomed. we should also congratulate the rapporteur who, with her sense of proportion and her feminine acumen, i would say, proposes and accepts possible improvements in the commission's view towards a greater approximation of the basic objective, which cannot but be the creation of truly comparable territorial units, with all that this means and implies, in order to obtain a universally acceptable european statistical system. the european citizen should not be given a frustrating answer to his reasonable questions about the injustices being perpetrated, such as, for example, 'this area is being strengthened, although it is in a better situation than yours because it is a nuts ii territorial unit, whereas yours is not'. the rationalization of all levels of the union's functioning should be our basic and constant aim.
in writing. - (el) the debate on territorial statistical units (nuts) is not a technical debate of a statistical nature, since regional statistics and the classification of regions on the basis of nuts constitute a stable source of reference for analysing the socio-economic developments of the regions, but also for identifying the regions eligible for structural aid. one of the main problems is the use of totally heterogeneous political-economic units as the basis for the collection and processing of statistical data. this choice is certainly not accidental, and becomes even more suspicious when it is linked to the fact that, as the report rightly points out, the commission is 'considerably flexible' in applying the criteria for the integration of a region at nuts 1, 2 and 3 levels, using them as it sees fit and according to the circumstances. it is significant that the nuts 3 administrative subdivision is used to allocate objective 2 aid, which helps to strengthen half the union's population while dramatically reducing funding for the less developed regions, for which this aid is supposed to be intended. during the period 1994-1999 (the second delors package), 51 per cent of the eu population received aid. of this 50%, only 25% corresponded to objective 1, which funds nuts 2 level regions. finally, of this 25%, 16.4% concerned objective 2. for example, rich regions have benefited from significant aid through the use of a small administrative subdivision (nuts 3), which has caused a glaring distortion in the implementation of regional policy. in the end, it is the rich regions that are favoured, as they absorb a large part of the structural funds, which are already meagre and insufficient. some countries are particularly favoured because the nuts 3 administrative subdivisions are very small compared to those of other countries. for example, in greece, such an administrative unit (nomós) has an area of 2 950 square metres, compared with 3 162 in portugal and only 758 in germany. this illustrates once again the dysfunctions, inequalities, irregularities and hypocrisy of eu regional policy, as a large part of the funds allocated to structural aid are 'recycled' and returned to the more developed member states. this practice decisively restricts the possibility of pursuing a national development policy, which must necessarily operate within the narrow and ill-conceived framework of community models. as a result, the much-vaunted objective of convergence between poor and rich regions is not being achieved, and increasingly marked divergences are emerging, not only between the northern and southern member states, but also between regions within the same member state. this is confirmed by the data, with greece being a black example. the only thing that has been achieved is to buy the consciences and create mechanisms that promote the interests of the big monopolies, which have benefited to the highest degree from community programmes. although the rapporteur's report does not go into the details, it does address a number of the serious problems that arise, which is why we will support it.

the lamassoure report, mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, argues that turkey has the right to become a member of the european union. that's the main message. i, for my part, believe that this is not only a right of turkey, it is also a vital need of this country. turkey has historically made an admirable effort to reach out to europe. an effort that began not today, but long before. turkey's efforts to modernize itself have been underway since the late 19th century. we greeks, as turkey's neighbours and, may i say, as turkey's historical opponents, know this better than anyone else. we know from our own experience how difficult it is to overcome the tradition of eastern despotism and move into a modern society. turkey has been particularly heavy in this tradition because it has been at the top of the pyramid of this despotism in our region. turkey, mr president, had the historic accident of coming out virtually untouched - not untouched, of course, because it suffered so much - but it was not defeated in either the first or the second world war. sometimes it's good luck to lose wars. we must not forget that france and germany, the core of the european union, were both defeated, one at the beginning and the other at the end of the second world war, and so i believe they learned that the power of arms and dynamic conflict is not the way to survive in the modern world. the tragedy of september 11th taught us, i believe, that the modern world needs reconciliation to survive -- not of course reconciliation with terrorists, but reconciliation in tensions between us -- precisely to isolate terrorists and to establish a world that is safe and just. turkey is a european country in the third world which should learn the lesson that france and germany have learned: that to move forward we must stop fighting in the present day the wars of the 1920s or the wars of the 1940s. i believe that turkey has constructed both an internal mechanism and a system of external relations dominated by the idea of imposition. in this way, it also tyrannises its own people and we have the examples in this terrible story of prison suicides and many other things. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force. it is trying to do so and we must help it and welcome it into the european union to the extent that it is making efforts to reach out to us.
mr president, the report acknowledges certain obvious truths. it's a fact. but at the same time, he's making findings that don't match reality. it sees progress that does not exist, it extols the famous 'national programme' and some individual reforms that are, in fact, ashes in the eyes of the world to hide the preservation and, in some respects, the deterioration of the situation for the popular strata. we had warned that the helsinki decision would not help democratization but would help to dismantle the regime. the military continues to play the decisive role in political life. the workers' movement is being treated in the most brutal way. the arrests - over 10 000 of them are political prisoners - the torture, the murders, the inhuman conditions in prisons, which are praised in the report, are on the agenda. for 370 days the hunger strikers continue their fight to the death, with dozens of dead. the death penalty was abolished only for convicted criminals. but not for the fighters for democracy and freedom. the report calls these fighters extremists and the turkish regime terrorists, while the kurdish people are deprived of their basic rights, the occupation troops remain in cyprus after a barbaric invasion 27 years ago, and the report says nothing. the key point of the report, however, is paragraph 29, which extols turkey's contribution to the fight against terrorism, apparently by persecuting kurdish fighters, and its peace efforts in the balkans, apparently meaning its open support for the kla criminals, and urges it to facilitate the implementation of the nato agreement. in conclusion, mr president, may i say that my feelings of brotherly love and solidarity with the peoples of turkey do not allow me to vote in favour of this report. interests of sovereign multinationals, which already monopolise certain areas of international trade, making them a nightmare for developing countries, but also for workers, whose rights are being eroded. the big countries favour the interests of their multinationals at the expense of the less developed countries, and remain silent in the face of the constant demands of these countries to obtain the cancellation of their debts. in the agricultural sector, the necessary restructuring must be carried out as quickly as possible so that land is concentrated in a few hands, so that it forms a link in an entire chain controlled by multinationals. in addition, the liberalisation of trade and the abolition of customs duties and subsidies are having a serious impact on agricultural production, with the result that agricultural income is falling, rural areas are declining and unemployment is increasing. agricultural production in greece and other countries is being sacrificed to protect the industrial products of the countries of central and northern europe and to enable them to better position themselves on international markets. we believe that in the new round of negotiations the eu will not defend the interests of the peoples but will try to claim a greater share for the european monopolies, which compete with the other imperialist organizations of the united states and japan. the rules governing international economic relations and trade must serve the interests of workers. they must not infringe basic social rights and must contribute to the development of less developed countries, respect the environment and the right of every people to exercise control and decide for themselves about their future. the wto is by its very nature an imperialist construct, concerned only with maximising the profit of capital and securing safer and more flexible means for capital, in particular trade and stock exchange capital, to flow unhindered, with the result that large sections of the world's population are impoverished and pollution and environmental destruction are constantly increasing. in this world dominated by the new order, where the law of the jungle prevails at all levels, where the big fish eat the small, the people increasingly understand that they have no other choice but to oppose and counterattack. the movement against 'globalisation' and the wto, despite its heterogeneity and polymorphism, constitutes a first counterattack against the dictatorship of monopoly capital and its economic interests. it sends a message of opposition and counterattack to millions of workers, the unemployed, small and medium-sized farmers, to all the victims of the world capitalist order that the wto wants to impose. the members of the communist party of greece voted against this resolution because, in essence, it is in line with the plans of big business to introduce unbridled liberalisation, to further plunder the poor countries, to ruin the countryside and to call into question the achievements of workers and the rights of peoples all over the world.
in writing. - (el) i must admit that our rapporteur, the german christian democrat elmar brok, does not mince words. both the motion for a resolution and the explanatory statement are characterised by a militaristic and aggressive cynicism with regard to the content of the cfsp (common foreign and security policy) and the esdp (european security and defence policy). the rapporteur therefore considers that a common political will to take operational action has emerged, especially since the second half of 2001, and he cites the cases of the middle east and fyrom as examples of successful autonomous eu intervention in 'crisis management'. the report then makes a long list of the instruments and structures which the eu now has at its disposal to undertake the 'meritorious' work of 'conflict prevention' and 'crisis management' (which the eu will of course be sure to bring about) all over the globe: a rapid intervention force whose primary objective is to create, by 2003, a european army with an initial strength of 60,000 men; measures for the rapid disbursement of funds; non-military - but armed - management of crises (through the creation of a force of 5 000 police officers); (new) situation centre and planning and early warning unit (both for solana), etc. russia, ukraine, moldova, africa, latin america, etc. are not only seen as neighbours but also as strategic partners. the avowed aim is to impose a policy serving imperialist interests, to get these countries to accept the rules of the capitalist market and, of course, ... to respect human rights in the sense that the eu understands them. in addition, in the name of 'global responsibility for peace, stability and sustainable development', the need for the eu to deploy its strategic plans in iran, china, korea and indonesia is emphasised. in other words, the brok report cynically highlights the imperialist nature of the eu, which, out of necessity, wants to promote its interests by a remarkable collaboration with the united states and by exploiting the means of nato. in fact, the report is very clear about the division of roles between the two imperialist poles, the us and the eu: the eu will act with the us if possible, but also without the latter if they are not willing or not yet ready to do so. what fits perfectly with this position is the attitude of fully supporting the united states in the new imperialist war in afghanistan and, more generally, in eurasia, under the pretext of fighting international terrorism which, like a deus ex machina, now provides, after the dissolution of the ussr and the warsaw pact, the "alibi", the "long-awaited adversary" to the imperialists, so that they can justify the arms race, the limitation of the rights of our peoples, the polymorphic fortification of their system and their interventions of all kinds - military and non-military - everywhere in the world. the spirit behind the report is reflected in the statement made by mr brok to the european parliament's plenary assembly at the presentation of the report: 'if we want to be convincing and credible in our foreign policy, we must prove every day that we are prepared to impose by force of arms what cannot be done by other means ...'. who today can doubt the marxist theory that war is in the nature of capitalism and, even more, of imperialism? that is why, mr president, the people cannot afford to rest. a peaceful front at national, european and world level against the imperialist warlords is the only prospect of survival for humanity.
mr. president, i apologize for my absence. the commission's proposal is a step in the right direction, but it does not change the situation in the sheep and goat sector. we welcome, for example, the proposal to increase the premium and the special aid, which have not been increased since 1993 and have therefore been wiped out by inflation. the proposal to make the conditions for payment of the additional aid more flexible is also positive. the proposal to maintain the difference between the subsidies for mixed production of meat and milk is not a positive one. the commission's proposal is based on the fact that the milk producers in the mixed production units have additional income from milk production. but it compares different things, because the meat-producing units, which are mainly in the northern countries, are large enterprises which provide their owners with a satisfactory income and profit, whereas the mixed production units, which are mainly in the mediterranean countries, are family farms in the most arid areas, which do not provide their owners with a sustainable income, with the result that these areas decline over time and become deserted. the livestock quotas in my country for each holding have not been increased since 1989-91. the proposal accepts the logic of quotas, and even tries to embellish it with management proposals, when the european union's self-sufficiency in goat and sheep meat is 80% and when goat and sheep farming is developing in the poorest regions of the european union, which usually have no other professional solution, and goat and sheep farmers are the poorest social group in the european union. if there was indeed a desire to support the income of these regions and sheep and goat farmers, quotas should be abolished, subsidies increased and other additional incentives given. such a policy would prevent the desertification of mountains and desertification of the majority of the regions, while at the same time ensuring the european union's self-sufficiency in sheepmeat and goatmeat.

commission. – energy drinks were discussed at the 84th meeting of the standing committee on foodstuffs on 19 september 2001. as noted, two member states have reported deaths in their countries associated with the consumption of those products. in neither case, however, was there any evidence that the consumption of energy drinks was the cause of death. both member states have commissioned their own research, investigating the available data on the effect of these drinks on health. recently another member state produced a list of incidents linked to the consumption of such products, but without any substantiating evidence. none of the member states concerned took any steps to restrict or prohibit energy drinks in their own territory. the standing committee noted a need for further information on the potential effects on health of ingredients in the products and the scientific committee for food is examining data submitted by a manufacturer of such products regarding the safety of one of the products in question. according to information, further studies are nearing completion. at the above mentioned meeting of the standing committee on foodstuffs, the commission repeated its request to member states to submit information on the safety of products on their markets. the commission understands the concerns that have been expressed and it therefore intends to work with member states to look at the issue in depth and identify the appropriate course of action to ensure a high level of protection for consumers. so-called energy drinks are not covered by any specific legislation and are not subject to an approval procedure at community level. in the absence of community legislation, member states may apply national rules without prejudice, of course, to the provisions of the treaty. overall, member states should ensure that products placed on their markets are safe.
i am grateful for your answer, commissioner. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply, but i would also like to express my disappointment at the content of the reply. the commissioner, as you are well aware, is talking about a public health issue and i would remind you that in these cases we do not need proof, but simple indications, and especially when we are talking about the public health of young people, such as those who consume energy drinks. in particular, in my country there have been five cases where clearly these people had symptoms directly related to the three active substances contained in these drinks. i would ask you to repeat to me - because it has happened in the past and then community legislation prohibited my country from banning this substance - whether this drink could indeed be banned at national level, because the initiative taken by the committee responsible in my country was opposed on the basis of the principles of internal trade.

the sixth framework programme of the european union, mr president, covers topics relating to high priority areas, but also to areas of pioneering research from which we expect great scientific leaps. i want to focus your attention on the groundbreaking field of human genome research. this research is expected to have future applications in four areas of medicine: first, hereditary diseases; second, the most common killer diseases, namely cancer, cardiovascular and neurodegenerative diseases; third, understanding the mechanisms of different diseases, leading to new, more effective treatments and drugs; and fourth, disease prevention. new knowledge in human genetic material will help us understand the areas of the genetic potential that are vulnerable and vulnerable to environmental challenges and pressures, as well as to our life habits, so as to enable the development of new strategies for the prevention, diagnosis and treatment of the diseases i have mentioned. stem cell research may also have potential applications in treating diseases such as parkinson's, alzheimer's, leukemia, and many others. however, research in these areas should be limited to avoid ethical problems. that was the aim of the amendment i tabled with my colleagues nisticò and purvis. this amendment allows the development of this groundbreaking research which i have described, while maintaining all the balances so that it does not create ethical problems. so we look forward to the promising applications of this research in medicine at some point in the future. i would like to believe that the european parliament will vote in favour of this line which sets out and maintains the right balance between progress and ethics.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we are dealing with a very important text, the content of which will not only determine the funding of research by the european union, but will also set guidelines and practices for all the member states, and especially for those which, like greece, do not have their own national research programme. a recognized weakness of research in the european union is low funding. the commission's proposal for a global budget, however, does not meet the need for a bold increase in funding and, unfortunately, the committee on industry, although adding activities, did not increase the budget. the second serious issue is orientation. the plan we are considering leaves little room for free inquiry, which will not produce immediately exploitable results, but will contribute to the development of knowledge and provide the basis for applications in the coming decades. in this respect, we consider that the proposal by the committee on industry to reduce expenditure on so-called priority 8 is not in the right direction. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. the third big issue is who gets the funding. the structure of the proposed programme is such as to benefit businesses directly and indirectly, since the guidelines are adapted to their needs. in our view, research by companies should be financed from their profits and not from the public budget. we are also concerned that small research groups will have difficulty in obtaining funding, although the amendments tabled by the committee on industry will make this easier. a note on the euratom programme: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy resources are not used for the production of nuclear fuel. in this respect, i welcome the fact that the committee on industry is proposing an increase in funding for the iter reactor. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. public funding of research is not the right way to solve these kinds of problems. it would be a serious omission on my part, mr president, if i did not thank commissioner busquin and his staff and, of course, our rapporteur mr caudron and all the shadow rapporteurs for the enormous work they have done.
the sixth framework programme for research is a serious and fundamental project of the european union in order to achieve its strategic objectives, namely to become the most competitive region in the world based on the knowledge economy in the next ten years. it also allows it to benefit from the progress made in other parts of the world and, at the same time, to contribute to the development of humanity. we must all rise to this challenge at european and national level. this is about boldly funding research in all areas, coordinating it at european level, as opposed to the fragmentation that is happening today, and spreading it throughout the economic and social fabric. the achievement of our political and development objectives depends precisely on the exploitation of the results of research for the benefit of health, the quality of life of citizens, full employment and our social cohesion. i am concentrating my intervention on research in the social field. the sustainability of our social model is the first condition for our development. the social agenda we have adopted and are implementing is the basis of this policy. economic and social developments have brought a dead end to our economy and society. demographic ageing, high rates of poverty and social exclusion, the high number of accidents at work, the need to successfully integrate large groups of citizens such as the roma, the integration of people with disabilities into the labour market, the continuing need to facilitate women's access to the labour market, the new risks to workers' health, the growing multiculturalism of european society require scientific research on which our policy will be based in the coming years. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. finally, i would like to stress the need for public information and consultation on all our research objectives. research in all fields, from biotechnology to social research, is far from the people, confined to laboratories or researchers' offices. citizens are unable to read not only scientific journals but also the major newspapers. we must make a systematic effort to inform.
in writing. - (el) the opening up of port services to competition, as promoted by the commission, will have a number of serious and negative consequences: (a) on the quality of port use and standards applied, because of competition and the employment of unskilled staff; (b) on the working conditions and future prospects of port workers, by endangering their health and safety, interrupting their vocational training and weakening their skills; (c) safety of maritime transport and the environment, due to the general deterioration of the services provided. although the resolution by the committee on transport makes some improvements with the amendments that have been voted, it shares the philosophy of the commission, in particular by reducing the scope of control by the public authorities, which is why i voted against it.
in writing. - (el) the general title of the community directive under consideration conceals a significant step forward in the privatisation of ports, and more particularly of services such as the steering, towing and mooring of vessels, the transhipment and storage of goods, and passenger traffic. the aim is not to improve the port services provided, but to sell a public sector back to capital once again and to deliver a functioning infrastructure to private monopolies to exploit and profit from. the inevitable consequences will be the dismantling of the status and achievements of the workers and the tragic decrease in safety levels for the workers, the passengers of the boats, but also the services provided. i would like to stress at the outset that we are categorically opposed to the directive, but also to the jarzembowski report, and that we reject them as a whole. we refuse to fall into the logic of taking privatisation as an inevitable fact and trying to save certain services. on the other hand, we strongly defend the public nature of port services and ports, functionally integrated into a global development project that will harness the capacities of each country, support the economy and employment and ensure vital safety in ports and at sea. these are extremely sensitive issues for greece, which has large archipelagos of islands, but also the aegean area, which is sensitive from an economic, social and national security point of view. moreover, it should be noted that only if they retain their public character can multi-purpose ports play their role effectively, as is the case, for example, with the port of piraeus, which supports and serves shipbuilding. what we're trying to do is more than just annoying and provocative. the port itself is being intervened in and prevented from doing what any other company does, which is to operate as a single entity. we are imposing dismemberment and internal competition between the services of the same operator ! at the same time, the directive talks about conditions of 'public service', which it delivers to the private sector! the communist party of greece is fighting with the workers to unify and make the port services 100% public, so that they can function, develop and become interwoven with other sectors of the economy. reasons of proper functioning, safety, coordination and development require support for a single, unified and vertically integrated service whose activities support and support each other. otherwise, the smooth and efficient functioning of the sector is jeopardized, all coordination is undermined and destroyed, and the pandora's box is opened in the field of security, which is now governed by the laws of profit and competition and is left to the will of the companies. that is why we voted against the jarzembowski report.

mr president, the prohibition of state aid to public enterprises, mainly shipbuilding, the refusal to write off the debts incurred by their previous private owners and the almost free handing over of them to private individuals are the instruments of the european union's policy, which, with the complicity of our governments, has led to the shrinking of this strategically important sector. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's trade policy is not distorted. the blow struck by the seventh directive was devastating for greece's highly developed shipbuilding industry. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted by the introduction of a new fishing fleet, and that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted by the introduction of a new fishing fleet. today, it is hypocritically proposed that the previously forbidden aid should be given to the now private shipyards as their only means of survival. the european union's commitment to serving big business is once again on display. the committee believes that the only viable solution is to create a single public body that can face international competition and also serve employment and the economy.
the council has repeatedly expressed its concern about the phenomenon of illegal immigration and recalls, in this connection, the conclusions of the european councils of tampere, santa maria de feira and nice. as regards the action it has taken and is taking in this area, the council would refer you to the answers it has already given to numerous questions from members of the european parliament on illegal immigration. the council recalls that the helsinki european council decided to consider turkey as a candidate country which is entitled to join the union on the basis of the same criteria as those applicable to the other candidate countries. the european council in helsinki provided, and i quote, 'that in order to intensify the work to bring turkish legislation and practices into line with the acquis, the commission is invited to develop a process of analytical examination of the acquis'. in accordance with the conclusions of the feira european council, the commission will report to the council on the progress of work to prepare the analytical review of the acquis with turkey. the council formally adopted the accession partnership for turkey on 8 march 2000. in accordance with the accession partnership, turkey has adopted a national programme for the adoption of the acquis which sets out the policy framework, the timetable for legislative work and the administrative and budgetary requirements necessary for turkey to adopt the acquis. in line with the practice established for all candidate countries, the commission will assess this national programme for the adoption of the acquis of turkey in the 2001 regular report on the basis of the accession partnership. the european union will closely monitor the practical implementation of the various measures announced in this framework. this national programme on the acquis contains various measures in the field of justice and home affairs. the main objectives pursued in 2001 were the strengthening of border controls and the harmonization of visa legislation and practices with the union acquis, as well as the adoption of the migration acquis, inter alia to prevent illegal immigration. the council reiterated its concern at the massive influx of illegal immigrants from or transiting through turkey into greek territory. the union requested turkey to implement the relevant measures set out in the eu report of november 2000. the commission is also prepared to provide financial assistance to turkey to continue the implementation of the recommended approach in accordance with the accession partnership. in this connection, the council noted with interest turkey's intention to lift in the medium term the geographical reservation made in connection with the 1951 geneva convention relating to the status of refugees. the council also engaged in an active dialogue with countries of origin of asylum seekers and/or illegal immigrants in the framework of the high level group on asylum and migration. this group, set up by the council in december 1998, as i said in reply to another member's question on the canary islands, is therefore responsible for drawing up action plans for certain countries. in drawing up these action plans, the group adopted a comprehensive, cross-cutting approach, taking into account aspects of foreign policy, economic development and the management of migration flows. among the countries selected for such action plans is iraq, as a country of origin and transit for immigrants. turkey, because of its geographical location, is obviously an important transit country. in the context of the action plan for iraq, the council has already started a dialogue with turkey in order to implement the european union action plan for iraq of 26 january 1998. one of the measures in the action plan is the negotiation of a transit agreement with turkey.

the rapporteur. i am sorry, mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, but in the case of this draft directive i cannot contribute to the harmony between the two institutions. i am sorry, but i shall be recommending that the commission's proposal be rejected. as you know, cigarettes are not a tax-exempt commodity. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. it's up to 70 to 80 percent of the price of a cigarette. the commission is now proposing a minimum flat-rate tax of eur 70, complementary to the system based on the proportional taxation currently in force. the argument is that the tax burden differs from country to country in the union, whereas it should converge. that's the first inaccuracy. it is not true that there is a tax divergence, it is true that there is no particular convergence, but there is no divergence. there is a certain convergence in cigarettes, a convergence which is greater than in many other products, and this is confirmed by an independent study by the european parliament's directorate-general for research, which was of course not written to serve my proposal. why should the commission be concerned with taxation? the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud, and it is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat the smuggling of tobacco products into the community. but to eliminate the effects of the price difference, the price difference itself must be eliminated. the commission has accepted that it is not known whether convergence of taxation will lead to convergence of prices. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission proposal also makes no provision for the harmonization of value added tax. the variation in value added tax between member states is as high as 100%. how can taxation be harmonized when there is no parallel harmonization of value added tax on cigarettes? the illegal trade is indeed illegal if it is the trade of taking cheap cigarettes from one country of the union where they have paid their tax and selling them in another country of the union where they are more expensive, so if this is illegal - in my opinion it is not, it is the normal functioning of the single market - but to the extent that it is illegal or if the tax has not been paid as in the case of england, then the situation will not change for that country, which is indeed suffering from cigarette smuggling - 50% of cigarettes smoked in england have become illegal cigarettes precisely because taxation has raised their prices - so england is not going to be affected by the situation because prices in the netherlands or france, where the smuggling is feared, will not be affected by the commission's proposal. on the contrary, prices will rise in the southern member states and, in particular, if we admit the applicant countries to the european union, when we do, which i hope we will, price increases in those countries must reach 200 to 400% under this directive. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter, and whether it is not aware that the commission has already taken a decision on the matter. the public health argument is also made that the increase in the price of cigarettes reduces smoking. we don't know that. how do we know this thing? because we have the statistics... but the statistics are based on legal cigarettes. the increase in the price of cigarettes does curb legal smoking, but it encourages illegal smoking, and in england the mafias are rampaging and have completely dismantled the cigarette market for that very reason. in addition, commissioner, i would say that the council has not shown parliament the necessary respect and tact, because a week before we give our opinion it presents us with a political agreement with which it is trying to blackmail us. i do not think that is the right way for our democratic institutions to function, and if that is the way our parliament is going to function, then why did we win the cold war? and i heard in your last speech that you are a staunch liberal. i am surprised that you are supporting a measure which in the economic sphere only benefits the large monopolies, for reasons which i cannot explain to you, but you know very well that this is the case, and in the political sphere you are supporting a measure which is very similar to totalitarianism. i think that such differences between the theory we advocate and the practice we follow should make you think.
mr president, this is one of the times when i agree with the rapporteur, mr katiforis, not because we speak the same language or have the same nationality, but because we agree as what is called a common mind. i am sorry, but common sense is absent from this proposal for a directive, which we also propose to reject as a whole. if it is not rejected, we all risk being exposed because the adoption of such a directive would have the exact opposite of the results sought by the council. i would also like to point out that it would be very useful if the council were present instead of being absent, as is common sense. the council says it is seeking to combat tax evasion and cigarette smuggling. if we raise taxes, then we increase tax evasion. it is well known that the higher the taxes, the more attractive the tax evasion becomes. if we raise the price of cigarettes, as proposed, then we will certainly increase the smuggling of cigarettes because in this way we will make legal cigarettes more expensive than illegal ones. so we're going against his wishes. the council also tells us that it is seeking to protect public health, which will be achieved by cutting smoking because cigarettes will be more expensive. i hope that the commission will take the necessary steps to ensure that the council of ministers is able to give its opinion on the matter. at best, it will not change anything about cigarette consumption. in the worst-case scenario, low-income smokers will be forced to resort to cheaper, and therefore more harmful, cigarettes. is that what we're trying to do? good for us! the proposals in the proposed directive are also unfair because they first of all softly limit the application to five, mainly southern, states. the other ten are unaffected because they are already at the top. the second is that, like these five countries, four of which are in the south, the cost of living will rise without improving incomes and inflationary pressures will be reinforced. i would also like to ask all of us: what do the candidate countries think of this proposed directive?
