in writing. - (el) the increasing energy consumption, combined with the decline in fossil fuel sources and deposits, makes energy supply a particularly important issue in the internal and external policy of the eu and the member states. the chichester report, while stressing the need to save energy, increase energy efficiency and develop efficient transport systems, puts its hopes for a solution to the problem in market liberalisation and competition on the one hand and control of supplying countries on the other. this approach serves the wider interests of imperialism. it does so at the expense of workers and, more generally, citizens, both in the supplying and the consuming countries, who are sacrificed on the altar of the profits of multinational companies which provoke and exploit crises, provoke wars, in order to control the regions of the planet which are of interest from the energy point of view. energy policy, as regards the exploitation of domestic energy sources, obeys the same injunctions, with adverse effects on the interests of the lower classes, on development, on the guarantee of quality, safe and ecologically acceptable energy for the whole population. the problem is aggravated by the liberalisation of the energy market and the privatisation of generation units and transmission networks. we believe that the strategic energy sector (supply of raw materials, production, transport networks) should be publicly owned and subject to national programming which will be periodically adjusted to take account of national needs and international conditions. energy policy must ensure the supply of cheap energy for the needs of the lower classes, guarantee the safety of workers and inhabitants, and ensure the protection of the environment. these must be the main objectives, over and above all economic factors. transport policy must be reoriented towards the use of public transport, renewable energy sources must be strengthened and encouraged, and research must be carried out into more cost-effective production systems and more economical, less polluting consumption systems. it is essential that the eu and the member states meet environmental protection requirements, and failure to reduce emissions to the levels set by the kyoto agreement is a bad pretext. the major problems of climate change, the ozone hole, etc., cannot be solved at the expense of the development of the poorest countries, at the expense of workers, or by fatalistic acceptance of the destruction of our planet. the workers in europe and the rest of the world, the two billion people who do not have access to electricity, are demanding and fighting for an alternative energy policy, opposed to imperialist interests and choices, against the monopoly capital that owns the energy sector. a policy which will respect and protect the environment, a policy which will create the basis for human living conditions. for the reasons stated above, we as members of the european parliament from the communist party of greece voted against the chichester report.
in writing. - (el) the report fully endorses the commission communication, the logic of which is to consider a natural resource - water - as a commodity, but also as an essential means of implementing other harmful sectoral policies, such as agricultural policy. this logic and the proposals which give it substance meet with our categorical opposition. the report focuses on what it calls 'pricing to promote the sustainability of water resources' by recovering the costs of water services and limiting water consumption and use precisely because of the price increase. at the same time, the report is driven by the familiar 'polluter pays' logic, which leads to new incentives and new profits for capital. under the hypocritical pretext of allegedly ensuring the sustainability of water resources, the real ones who will pay, and even pay very dearly, for the consequences of the proposed measures are consumers, mainly farmers in southern europe, since both the commission report and the communication in question indicate that they are the biggest consumers of water, those who pay for water below its 'real price'. this policy will lead to an increase in the cost and a worsening of the disappearance of small and mediumsized farms, which are already suffering the devastating effects of the common agricultural policy. in a typical formula, the report divides the cost into financial, environmental and resource costs, considering that only financial costs have been taken into account until now. thus, and according to the report, the cost of building and operating a dam on a river, due to excessive (agricultural) levying, "can be charged to the users"... or, if deemed necessary, the cost of purifying polluted water (for example, by fertilisers), and then it is the farmers who have to pay it. we oppose any attempt to treat water as a 'commercial' product. we absolutely deny the commission the right to claim to be the protector of water resources, which, under the pretext of 'encouraging sustainable use', once again 'water to the mill' of private interests by offering them the opportunity to enter and act in a purely public sector of general interest. this attempt is just another chapter in a disastrous community policy which will be carried out to the detriment of consumers and farmers, while at the same time serving as a bulwark to promote other policies, such as the dismantling of the agricultural sector. the protection and good management of water resources cannot be achieved through repressive tariff policy mechanisms and recovery measures to the detriment of consumers. the only beneficiaries of this policy will be certain large companies which, by selling their 'environmental profile' at a high price, will still find a good opportunity to increase their profits tenfold.
madam president, while congratulating the rapporteur on his work, i would also like to thank him for his cooperation in accepting the necessary amendments to the whole project. the amendment of this directive follows the amendment of two other directives on collective redundancies and the transfer of undertakings, which have the common objective of protecting the interests of workers in the context of company restructuring. we must not forget that. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. secondly, the directive leaves open the question of the community definition of wages. despite the difficulties involved, the adoption by the fifteen of a minimum common concept for the employed person is a necessary condition for the uniform application of the directive. otherwise, each country, having a wider or narrower concept of itself, varies the scope of the directive as much as it can. it's a spot we have to watch out for. the amendments proposed, which i support, include the extension of the scope of the directive to those employees who are treated as employees under national legislation, the so-called 'non-employed'. if we are not ready to extend the directive to selfemployed people, we could at least extend the application of the directive to those employees who are selfemployed and are considered to be employees under special legislation. secondly, the amendment is an inclusion in the concept of covered claims and compensation paid in the event of dismissals provided for by the international convention 173 and i do not see why we in europe cannot cover them. thirdly, it is the clearest redefinition of the concept of insolvency to cover all collective insolvency proceedings, and not just the case of the classic bankruptcy proceedings. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the application of the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. - (el) despite the good intentions of our rapporteur, we are concerned that, on the occasion of the year of the disabled (2003), large sums of money will be spent and many events will take place without any change in the situation of these people, mainly those from the poorest sections of society. we fear this because we see the hypocrisy of all this interest shown by the various eu institutions towards the infirm and disabled, when it is the policy of the european union itself that is responsible for this situation with capitalist restructuring, privatisation of health, social security, education, with the blows to the conquests of workers and social security. the first and most painful victims of this policy are the disabled. it hypocritically claims to promote "equal opportunities and the fight against discrimination". but does the child or young person from the poorest layers of society, who is exposed to pathogenic factors and accidents, have, first of all in terms of prevention, equal chances with those of the child or young person from the bourgeoisie? does the child of a multinational executive have the same chances of finding a job as the child of an unemployed person? it is impossible to combat the various discriminations that develop in capitalist society when the great discrimination between exploited and exploiters exists and prevails. what kind of fight against discrimination is this when, with the policies being pursued, poverty, unemployment and illiteracy are being sown? of the 38 million disabled people in the eu, 7.5 million are of school age. how many of these children have a school, a classroom and specialised means of education? in greece, only 7% of disabled children have a specialised school to go to, while 185,000 of these children are excluded from the educational process. but nowhere in the eu text, including the one we are debating, is there any mention of free public health, social security and specialised education, even if only for these people. nowhere is there any mention of the free provision of technical aids necessary for education, vocational rehabilitation, daily life. this situation will change when the people, the workers, but also the disabled, through their struggles, oppose the policy of "free competition and the laws of the market" until it is liquidated. it is in favour of these struggles that the communist party of greece votes.
- madam president, i also thank my colleagues, whose interest in human rights and the rule of law in the countries of the european union i welcome. i am also grateful for the opportunity to point out that in greece, as i believe in the united kingdom, all the rules, procedural and substantive, as well as those of human rights and the rule of law, are observed to the highest standard. in this sense, i believe that the judicial authorities of my country will be strictly fair and will observe all the provisions, whether of domestic law in greece or of international conventions. but in view of this and for information purposes only, i would like to inform the house of a number of things so that we know where we are. we are at a time when the issue of terrorism has taken on international dimensions. the greek detainees were arrested the previous day at another airport, they were told that they could not take pictures of military installations, even during operations or exercises, and, according to newspapers, their materials included surveillance of communications between warplanes or between planes and bases. then they found them at another airport. at another airport they were arrested, having been informed of what was happening. so as you can see, in a time of terrorism awareness, in a time when it is well known that all military bases in europe have similar bans, and once they were notified, all the room for flexibility or oversight was exhausted. i am sure that all the procedural provisions will be strictly observed, because greece is in the forefront of the 15 in respecting all the necessary guarantees for every citizen of any country who is on trial with it. i hope that the house has been informed of all that it needs to be informed.

- mr president, it may not be known to you but mrs fontaine will certainly have the following in mind. we have been bringing this matter to the european parliament for ten years and it concerns three greek seamen, members of the lower crew of a greek ship, who were arrested and sentenced to very heavy sentences in egypt for having 7.5 tonnes of drugs on board. to the glory of egyptian justice, the guilty for this crime were not the ship's owners, nor the ship's leadership, but the three poor people, members of the lower crew. the commission has already expressed its support for the two journalists and has asked for their release or transfer to greece. two and a half years ago, one of the three died in prison because of the unacceptable conditions of detention. a few days ago, another one died of, you know, tonsillitis. one more man alive with his health shattered. before he takes his last breath, let the bureau ask for his release.
- mr president, the scientific news of the last few days has created a dramatic backdrop to the presentation of the fiori report. but more important to me were the many, many letters i received from scientific academies, from medical companies and from patient organizations, urging us not to close the door on promising and very promising research. indeed, new insights in human genetics will help us understand the areas of our genetic potential that are vulnerable and vulnerable to environmental challenges and pressures, as well as to our life consciousness, so as to enable the development of new strategies for the prevention, diagnosis and treatment of disease. stem cell research, moreover, may potentially have applications in the treatment of diseases such as parkinson's, alzheimer's, leukemia and many others. however, research in these areas must be limited to avoid ethical, social and legal problems. this was the aim of the amendments which i tabled with other colleagues, as was done with the caudron report, to allow the development of the pioneering research i have described, while maintaining all the balances so that it does not create ethical problems. so we look forward to some time in the future and the medical advances that this research promises. i would like to believe that the european parliament will vote in favour of this line which sets out and maintains the right balance between progress and ethics. the text we are voting on must leave the door open to exploring new possibilities for relieving human societies as far as possible of the misery that diseases bring. it should not be dictated by confusion and fear or by a reaction to something we do not fully understand, but by the courage for new conquests that man has always shown since the age of prometheus.
- mr president, i think it is a tragedy for mankind that recent advances in biotechnology have reached a stage where the capitalist system still prevails. in the inhuman exploitation system in which we live today and where they are subordinated to the pursuit of profit, these discoveries are impossible to put at the service of humanity. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are not undermined. it will not be surprising to see the market for human eggs, embryos and stem cells expand, for example, or even to see new therapeutic options become as affordable as those that can afford them. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i can't help but think that life took a billion years to develop on our planet and evolved through the process of natural selection. now we have the ability to intervene in the evolutionary process, we can maybe see the immediate results, but we can't predict what will happen in a million years. and if that seems like a long time, i can assure you as an astronomer that the sun will be around for another 4.5 billion years. we are also faced with the degenerative phenomenon of patenting living organisms and human genes. our view is clear. no part of the human body, no cell, no molecule can be patented. we are also categorically opposed to the use of the genetic code by employers, insurance companies, secret services, etc. the fiori report contains interesting and useful views on a number of issues. in several articles it points out the risks arising from commercialisation, especially for the economically weakest groups of the population. however, it remains largely locked into the logic of competitiveness and is almost neutral on the legal question. the debate we are concluding today should be the beginning of a more general reflection and a catalyst for developing a more responsible attitude to these issues.
- mr president, when we decided about a year ago to set up the temporary parliamentary committee on human genetics and other technologies in modern medicine, we did not imagine that the day of the vote on this matter would coincide with the statements by heads of state, scientists, private companies and other interested parties. the announcement by advance cell technology of the creation through cloning of human embryos makes today's vote even more crucial. the discussions that preceded it showed minor divergences in important areas such as the protection of human rights, the inviolability of human dignity, the right to self-determination, the confidentiality of personal data, respect for persons with disabilities and the prohibition of discrimination against workers on the basis of their genetic criteria. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the matter. the commission also agreed that genetic information from living humans cannot be patented. the position of the temporary committee on the prohibition of funding for research related to reproductive cloning was also almost unanimous. but on the sensitive issue of therapeutic cloning, reflection and disagreement took up most of our time. the absence of an international framework and rules for the implementation of the relevant research, as well as the risk of causative or commercial creation of excess embryos, were some of the reasons for the strong reservations in this regard. it is clear that in this area it is not possible at present to secure a broad consensus and that further in-depth investigation is required by the continuation of the work of the temporary committee. i am sure that if we secure an international agreement and private and public bodies move into scientifically predetermined research areas on the basis of agreed protocols, the above-mentioned reservations will be removed. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european parliament is able to exercise its right to vote and to stand as a candidate in the elections to the european parliament.
in writing. - (el) the proposal on air safety does not deal with so-called terrorism. the fight against terrorism is simply used as a pretext for creating a collective psychosis of terrorism which will facilitate the acceptance of the anti-democratic and anti-social policies which are being introduced. at the same time, and under the same pretext, there is a great opportunity for profit through the promotion, for the adoption or enforcement, of "safety" measures in the form of very expensive equipment or services which passenger users are asked to pay after the cost has been passed on to them. in addition, efforts are being made to 'internationalize' and impose 'anti-terrorist measures' on all countries, which will be subject to controls by 'inspectors' whose mission is deliberately vague and not entirely clear. it is no coincidence that the proposal is being sought to have global legal effect - in the context of agreements between eu member states and other states - since the aim is to achieve the widest possible control and the strongest possible restriction of people's freedoms. a prime example is the new airline safety act, signed by president bush on 19 november, which requires foreign airlines to submit to the competent us authorities full details of their passengers travelling to the us. the united states requires airlines in many countries to submit lists in advance containing detailed information about each passenger, including first and last name, date and place of birth, passport number and place of issue, visa or residence permit number, and "any other information deemed necessary" for screening potential terrorists. at the same time, they warn that, if they do not comply, from 29 november onwards passengers on these airlines will be subjected by the us authorities, upon arrival, to strict checks and thorough searches of their luggage which may last for hours. this has a bitter taste of "cloaking", of repression and suppression of the free movement of citizens, of their rights, in a climate of "terrorist psychosis" in order to serve objectives foreign to security. we believe that the measures already in force are more than sufficient and do not call for additional measures. the manifestations of terrorism have social causes and their treatment is therefore social. the adoption of increasingly severe and ineffective measures of repression is a subterfuge and has other objectives than the fight against terrorism.
in writing. - (el) in the context of the euro-mediterranean agreement with egypt, the council must attach particular importance to the protection of community nationals who are faced with a lack of freedoms in that country. more concretely, the council must intervene in the case of the last survivor of the three greek seamen convicted some ten years ago for drug trafficking. two of them, despite their relatively young age, have already died in the intolerable conditions of egyptian prisons. the council must intervene to save the life of the last surviving greek seaman in egyptian jails; he's to serve the remainder of his sentence in greek prisons. in view of this human rights issue, which has rightly raised awareness in greek society, i abstained in the vote on the martin hugues resolution.
in writing. - (el) the group of the european united left, to which i belong, does not support the report of the committee on civil liberties and human rights on the european arrest warrant. the implementation of integration, but also the internationalisation of challenges such as terrorism and drugs, naturally require the creation of a single area of law, which must, however, be based on the consolidation and expansion of democratic rights. the introduction of the european arrest warrant, in the hasty and ill-considered manner in which it has been introduced, does not reflect the maturation of a process that has developed within the european union but rather the pressure of the united states; (b) is not limited to the pressing challenges of terrorism but extends to a wide range of activities that are extraneous to terrorism; (c) occurs without consultation with civil society but through coercive procedures; (d) it stiflingly restricts the legal space of the member states. in view of these data, the possibility of creating an authoritarian european structure with marked democratic deficits is worryingly looming.
in writing. - (el) for some time now, and even more recently, the european union has become an industry producing legislation that undermines democratic freedoms (schengen information system, extension of europol's powers, european public prosecutor, restrictions on the entry and movement of immigrants). it is symptomatic that, just a few days before 9/11, a report was adopted on the taking of initiatives in the fight against terrorism. the european parliament is now asked for its opinion - which is not binding on the council or the commission - on the two framework decisions on terrorism and the european arrest warrant. there is no doubt that the abominable events of 11 september were used as a pretext to accelerate the implementation of long-standing political decisions. the perpetrators and instigators of these attacks have given the us and european imperialists a great gift: they have provided them with a pretext to settle accounts on a global scale and within their borders. the communist party of greece, aware of its responsibility for the freedoms and rights of the people, and knowing from bitter experience that there is a terrifying similarity between many of the proposed provisions and the laws which in the past sent thousands of greek activists to prison, deportation and even to the firing squad, has a duty to reveal the true intentions of the eu leaders. it should be noted that everything that is understood by 'terrorist offences' already falls under the criminal legislation in force in all states. the real aim is to terrorize any movement of resistance and political disobedience, but also parties that oppose and react forcefully to aspects or the whole of the policy of capitalist globalization. common forms of mobilisation, such as symbolic occupations, or the effects of such mobilisations, such as power or water cuts when the sectors in question go on strike, can easily be considered terrorist offences. cotton producers, desperate about eu policy, who demonstrate with their tractors on national roads, risk being labelled as terrorists and facing a minimum of 5 or 10 years in prison. we have reached the point where even the expression of an opinion is sanctioned. the framework decision on the european arrest warrant, of dubious legality, is a blow to the sovereign rights of the member states and is contrary to article 5 (2) and articles 6 and 7 of the greek constitution. with the abolition of double criminality, with the possibility of surrendering a national to another state, with the possibility that the state to which the accused has been surrendered may also extradite him (without the authorisation of the state which surrendered him) to a third state which is not a member state of the eu (for example, a kurd who is surrendered by greece to germany, which in turn extradits him to turkey without consulting greece), fundamental principles of criminal law are abolished, as are essential procedural rights and freedoms which are absolutely essential for the defence of the persons being prosecuted. with the anti-terrorism law and the european arrest warrant, the creation of a terrifying mechanism of repression has come to an end. it is clear that those who do so know full well that their policy, which is contrary to the interests of the people, at the level of the european union as well as at the global level, is provoking reactions among the working class. they expect these reactions to continue to increase and they fear the torrent of popular anger. what they don't know, or perhaps they don't want to admit, is that there is no force that can stop this torrent. the amendments tabled are intended, at best, to improve certain outrageous positions or to fill in gaps in the texts, but without changing the nature of the texts in any way. we cannot enter into such a logic. the only honest attitude, the only attitude which respects individual and collective democratic rights, is to reject both proposals as a whole. for the reasons stated above, we as members of the communist party of greece voted against the report.
in writing. - (el) for the second time in six months, the european parliament's plenary session has been asked to report on the draft law establishing eurojust. it comes on the same day as the debate on the european anti-terrorism law and the european arrest warrant. eurojust is an institution which will consist of one representative from each country (prosecutor, judge or senior police official) who will have very wide and uncontrollable powers in investigating and prosecuting. it will in fact be empowered to order the authorities of any state to open an investigation and prosecute, and even to participate in the investigations itself through the setting up of joint intervention groups. the member state must comply with its instructions, failing which it will be obliged to give express reasons for its refusal. in the previous debate, the argument put forward for the need to create eurojust was the fight against crossborder crime, in other words the fight against crimes in respect of which there could be a conflict of competence between at least two countries. it now seems that the leaders of the european union feel that they do not need any excuses. in the new text, this supreme principle will be able to apply even if the crime concerns only one country. of course, among the crimes that will fall within the competence of eurojust will be so-called terrorism. in carrying out its pious work, eurojust will be assisted by four different databases of sensitive personal data: the criminal records that each state keeps on its citizens, the schengen information system, the european judicial network and the europol files. the very long list of personal data includes items relating to 'racial or ethnic origin, political opinions, religious or philosophical beliefs or membership of trade union organisations, as well as data relating to health and sexual life'. this is in fact a legalisation of data storage through a european para-judicial body. it is undoubtedly a hyper-centralized and authoritarian institution which abolishes any notion of national sovereignty. it is also an attack on the famous functional independence of the judiciary, of which bourgeois democracy was so proud. there can be no illusions: the main objective of this whole legal arsenal is not the fight against common law crime. with the anti-terrorism law, the european arrest warrant and the european public prosecutor coming, efforts are being made to create a supranational repressive mechanism that will be used against democratic freedoms, and in particular against the popular movement when it opposes the dominant political and economic choices. for the reasons i have just mentioned, we as members of the communist party of greece voted against the report.
in writing. - (el) it is clear that the plans for the future of the eu are intended to serve the interests of big business even better and to establish the new imperialist order. they favour the pursuit of ultra-neoliberal policies, with all that this implies for the peoples. the development of a wider, more massive and more vigorous reaction of the workers and, more generally, of our peoples, is what is needed to bring this policy to a halt, to bring about a radical change of course. the prospect of europe's militarisation, of the erosion of national independence and popular sovereignty, of substantial limitations on the social and democratic rights of citizens, of a general offensive against the workers' movement and the living standards of the working people, remains at the heart of the objectives of the new reform pursued by the laeken european council and, subsequently, by the intergovernmental conference. the broad participation of national and european actors and bodies that is proposed does not reverse or stop this policy, but ensures accomplices in its implementation. the participation of all these actors does not change the substance of the debate, nor does it give a real possibility of intervention, since it will not, among other things, be deliberative but merely consultative. on the other hand, it offers the dominant forces the opportunity to continue to implement their projects under the veil of legality and a semblance of democracy. efforts are being made to mitigate the expected popular reaction, which has already been manifested in the previous treaty revisions, in maastricht, amsterdam and nice. this is why the same method as that used for the "charter of fundamental rights" is being tried again. but this method has been tested in practice and the results have shown that widespread participation has not only failed to consolidate and extend individual and social rights in response to the present demands, but that on the contrary these rights have been amputated at the behest of big capital, provoking strong popular reactions. the workers are fighting and will continue to fight to limit the harmful consequences of these projects and decisions, and in this fight they will take advantage of all the opportunities available. but they are not deceived or misled, their struggle is becoming more and more intense and their counter-offensive more and more effective and vigorous in favour of another europe, other popular institutions that will serve their own interests, peace and cooperation, on the basis of mutual benefit, real - and not only apparent - respect for all their individual and collective rights.

able to properly inform their customers. from the very beginning, the council encountered great difficulties in agreeing on a common position. most member states were opposed to this obligation and the positions of parliament and the council were quite far apart. at the time, with a view to reaching a compromise, the commission accepted the council's position which finally had the unanimous support of all the member states. this led to a difference in view between council and parliament after the second reading in april. i am pleased, however, that an acceptable solution has been found positive list of feed materials, i have already on several occasions outlined the commission's reservations. this concerns especially the deadline of presenting a proposal by the end of next year. this task is very complex. the commission has already launched a feasibility study on this issue. the call for tenders was published last june and the contractor is currently working on the matter and will also contact members of this parliament. i accordingly undertake to send the report on the outcome of this study to the council and parliament as soon as possible and in any case before 31 december, 2002. i cannot, however, guarantee that i will be in a position to present a legislative proposal on the establishment of a positive list. for this reason, it has been necessary for the commission to make a declaration on this subject.
madam president, the committee on economic and monetary affairs has considered how we should continue to work on this directive after we rejected the commission proposal in the last plenary by a considerable majority. according to the regulation, we have two months in which to deliberate and then come up with a reply. the economic committee has given me until monday to complete my negotiations with colleagues, the commission and the presidency. from what i see, there is no urgency in the matter because in any case the political agreement supposedly reached in council on the matter is not set to begin before july. there is therefore plenty of time for negotiation and for making contacts, and 15 january – when we shall reply – is not too far away. therefore, i see no particular need for urgent procedure and my proposal is that we reject the urgency request.
in writing. - (el) the policy pursued by the european union and the member states leads to the bankruptcy of hundreds of thousands of small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) each year, which are unable to counter the onslaught of monopoly capital. the signs of economic recession are becoming more and more evident, with smes the first victims, with the jobs thus lost which could in no way be compensated by the jobs, generally limited and short-term, which are linked to the episodic subsidies. the commission proposal merely serves the objective of strengthening the european venture capital market by introducing financial intermediaries which are not controlled or operate on the basis of the criterion of job creation. in most cases, smes do not receive direct funding, since applications are made to intermediaries, which are, as a rule, traditional 'venture capital and investment funds', which are guaranteed by the eib and receive loans and guarantees from it. so, in the end, no one outside the european investment bank knows which smes are being supported, which financing projects and which jobs will result. these intermediary financial institutions, which have contracts with the european investment fund, are primarily oriented towards seed investments in high-technology sectors such as information technology, communications and life sciences. the commission has already taken a number of measures to help smes, and it is not surprising that the commission has taken a number of measures to help smes. the enormous importance of smes to the economy of each country is thus ignored, since they are shown to be the backbone of the business sector in europe and the only ones to have created jobs in recent years. the uneven geographical distribution of this aid is also a clear sign of the widening regional disparities between the member states, since more than half of this funding (whether in the form of start-up aid or the guarantee mechanism) is allocated to france and germany, whereas, for example, in greece, this aid is nil, because 'the risk capital market is not developed'. we have no illusions about the nature of the eu's employment policy, or about the policy in favour of smes, which in fact does not exist, or about the interests it expresses. we therefore consider it hypocritical to refer to employment, the fight against unemployment and aid to small and mediumsized enterprises as part of the guidelines of this policy. the workers and the heads of small and mediumsized enterprises are opposing these choices with their struggles to overturn this policy and promote another that will take account of man and his new needs.
the commission. - mr president, mr alavanos' question refers to the fatal accident in perama, which was indeed a particularly tragic event, and i would like to express the commission's condolences to the families of the workers who were killed. to mr alavanos' question whether there are directives and accompanying measures for the safety of workers on tankers which should have been transposed into greek law, the answer is that there are neither new measures nor a new legislative framework which should have been transposed, and that framework directive 89/391, which covers both the shipbuilding and shipbuilding and repair industry, has been transposed into greek law in a way that covers working conditions in these cases as well. it should be noted that the greek authorities are investigating this accident and the european union awaits the final results of the investigation into the causes so that, in cooperation with the greek government, measures can be taken to prevent such accidents from happening again. the commission's strategy for health and safety is one of the european commission's priorities. at european level there is a huge number of deaths every year. the new strategy on health and safety is due to be published in 2002 and the report on the implementation of european legislation on health and safety at national level is currently being prepared. we are therefore preparing a european report which will assess the extent to which each country has implemented the health and safety standards by sector in the last year.
- commissioner, in connection with your last sentence, i would like to ask the following: does the report you have referred to contain any information on the way in which directive 89/391/eec is being implemented? i ask because it's not the first time there's been an accident. especially in perama, they've become a custom. in addition, i would like to ask you the following: i have studied the texts and, not being a very skilled legal expert in these matters, i have found other directives which, judging by their titles, seem to be related to the subject under consideration here. the commission has not yet received any information on the extent of the damage caused by the accident. do these instructions really have nothing to do with it? and in any case, since you said that you expect the latest from greece - and rightly so - does the commission not occasionally examine on its own initiative the situation, at least in very sensitive and marked areas such as perama?

the commission. - i would say that i fully share mrs cratza's concerns about the availability of comparable statistics on accidents at work, occupational accidents and occupational diseases at european level. as i said before, the european commission has set the strategy for health and safety as one of its priorities and the harmonised data of the statistical office will be the basis for the proper development of this strategy. since 1990, the commission has developed two harmonised statistical methodologies. the first is esaw, which concerns european data on accidents at work, with a reference year of 1993. the second is the eods, which refers to the statistics on occupational diseases with a reference year of 2001. it has also provided for a specific section on work-related health problems in the 1999 labour force survey. as regards accidents at work since 1993, the commission has annual data for 9 economic sectors. these data are from different national sources, include data from greece and are adjusted, after being obtained from the member states, by the statistical office, in order to estimate the total number of accidents and to ensure comparability at european level. as regards your comment on the figures provided by greece compared with other countries, the figures provided by greece relate to the ika and relate only to a part of accidents, as only those involving more than three days' leave from work are reported. the ika estimates that 39% of accidents are reported, a figure comparable to that of denmark, ireland and the united kingdom, and it should be borne in mind that the ika covers 45% of greek private employees. however, in 1999 we know that 43.4% in greece were employers, selfemployed and family workers, compared with the corresponding average at european level according to eurostat of 16.2%. in the context of the new community safety strategy, the commission will propose measures to improve the reporting of accidents at work and occupational diseases, which we believe will help to promote this strategy in all member states. i should like to stress, however, that it is up to governments to ensure that the national provisions transposing community directives are effectively implemented, particularly those relating to the obligation of employers to notify workers of accidents at work in their undertakings. the european commission has great difficulty in monitoring the situation at national and regional level, let alone at company level. it is therefore important for a country to put in place national health and safety control mechanisms.

commission. – slaughterhouses in greece are approved by the competent authorities under council directive 64/433 on health conditions for the production and marketing of fresh meat as amended by directive 95/23. 47 slaughterhouses are recorded as being approved for placing meat on the community market. the meat from the remaining operational slaughterhouses that is judged fit for human consumption can be marketed on the national territory. the last inspection mission on fresh meat carried out in greece by the commission's food and veterinary office was in september 2000 in relation to porcine meat. this mission identified serious wide-ranging deficiencies in the operation of official controls and production standards in this sector. as the honourable member is aware from the reply to his earlier question at the november session, there have been follow-up contacts with the greek authorities during 2001 in an attempt to resolve the problems found. to date the action taken has not been satisfactory and the commission remains very concerned about the situation. further high level meetings with the greek authorities are planned for early 2002 in an attempt to resolve the remaining shortcomings in food safety and veterinary control standards. with regard to waste water treatment, council directive 91/271 concerning urban waste water treatment foresees rules for slaughterhouses. waste water above a particular volume must meet conditions established in prior regulations and or specific authorisations by the competent authority since 31 december 2000. where slaughterhouses discharge directly into the public sewer, the general rules of the directive are valid. detailed information on the situation of industrial discharges in greece is under assessment.

played a major part. i think that doha has reminded us of the fundamental rule of the community's algebra, which is that the weight of the negotiators should be an exponential function of the union's solidarity. once again, this rule has proved true. but this solidarity was not the result of a sudden act of faith or an expression of blind trust in the two negotiators, whatever the qualities you have highlighted. in reality, this solidarity has been based on the substance of the union's negotiating position and on the balance, which is in some way intrinsic to our mandate, between liberalization and the setting of rules, between our general concerns for the northern countries and our openness to the concerns of the southern countries, between the specific values we want to defend as europeans and openness to a number of concerns of partners who do not necessarily share them. so, of course, we haven't achieved our goals one hundred percent. that is true, but as with any negotiation, we have reached a compromise, a compromise where we can all win, as it is not a zero-sum game. it is true, however, that in the social field, and i think neither franz fischler nor i have transparency, it is true for participation in decision-making by a much larger number of actors around the wto table, but i think we have probably reached the limit in doha of what the informal can the rapporteur. - madam president, the establishment of the community action programme in the field of public health is the first integrated project of the european union in the field of public health protection. the protection of public health is a major concern for the citizens of the union today. health must be a priority, beyond political or economic compromises, and cannot be considered a commodity. the programme which will replace the sectoral and piecemeal community health actions is a more integrated programme, mainly oriented towards disease prevention, and will contribute to the establishment of quality standards in the field of public health. the council's common position incorporated a substantial part of parliament's first-reading amendments, mainly because of the excellent interinstitutional cooperation developed between the community institutions. however, important elements of our proposals were not adopted in the common position or even part of the programme proposal was deleted by the council, leading to an amendment of the common position in the committee on the environment, whose members voted by a large majority for this recommendation for second reading. i would now like to make a few comments on the programme. the main aim of the programme is to strengthen cooperation between the member states in order to create added value from the synergy of community actions by developing three lines of action, which are the general objectives of the programme. the first is to improve information and knowledge for the development of public health. the second pillar is to strengthen the capacity to react rapidly and in a coordinated manner to health threats. i note here, as it is exactly three months since the terrorist attack of 11 september, that a category should be included dealing with health threats from physical, biological and chemical agents due to unforeseen events, e.g. accidents or inadequate infrastructure or machinery, human error or even terrorist acts. the third pillar is to address the determinants of health in all policies and activities through health promotion and disease prevention actions. the programme, which complements national policies, aims to achieve a high level of physical and mental health and greater health equity throughout the community. given the limited resources of the programme, it is necessary to give priority to health protection measures to support actions aimed at the prevention of more serious diseases and to practical actions, as well as to the need to develop an integrated and cross-sectoral strategy for health, in which all community policies and actions contribute to its protection and promotion. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. although there was almost complete agreement with the council on the objectives of the programme as well as on individual issues, there is still much debate about the coordinating format and budget of the programme. the european parliament considers that in order to coordinate and monitor effectively such a complex programme involving networks, data, various institutions, non-governmental organisations, there is a clear need for a coordinating body. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the environmental impact of the directive is not undermined. the council in its common position and the commission in its amended proposal have, of course, largely adopted parliament's position on the development of an effective system and mechanism for the coordination and monitoring of the programme. the wording they have chosen, however, in the confused form of structural arrangements, including a reinforced central core, is not the best and creates at least confusion and ambiguity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the budget is a matter of principle. the funding of the programme must be adequate. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the budget should therefore be increased in order to fully meet the needs and objectives of the programme and to cover, inter alia, the requirements of the implementation of the council's proposals on the preparation, establishment and operation of the appropriate structural arrangements, including the reinforced central core, as defined by the council in the second subparagraph of article 7.1. it is also essential to have flexibility to allow for the reallocation of resources. finally, strengthening the foundations of the european structure requires convergence not only in the strategic and political spheres of the economy, for example the single market and emu, but also in individual sectors such as health. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i therefore believe that the new programme is the beginning of such a process of convergence in the vital field of health.

in writing. - (el) i am afraid that the european union is taking a worrying course with the recent package of measures aimed at reducing the freedoms of citizens. unfortunately, europe is getting an arrest warrant before a constitution. in this context, the eu-us agreement on extradition in the context of the fight against terrorism is particularly worrying. under article 2 of the charter of fundamental rights, which was adopted by the european council in nice, france, on 7 december 2000, no one may be sentenced to the death penalty. in view of this article, it is unthinkable to cooperate with the united states on extradition, because the death penalty is applied there and the conditions for a fair trial are not met, after the military courts or the detention of hundreds of immigrants without a guarantee of a fair trial. even if extradition to the united states is on the condition that the united states "will not apply the death penalty", the death sentence can be pronounced by the court and remain unenforceable. in this way, through this agreement with the united states in the criminal field, the european union is reintroducing the death penalty through the window. that's a big step backwards for you. finally, i should like to point out that in afghanistan the united states applies the death penalty without any decision of its courts, and on a large scale. this is confirmed both by the statements made yesterday by the minister of defence, mr rumsfeld, who said: 'we are going to kill al-qaeda' and by the use of a 15,000 pound bomb called the 'daisy-cutter', which is the most powerful non-nuclear bomb of mass destruction. it is one thing to exterminate the taliban and al-qaeda and to punish by example those responsible for the crime of 9/11, but it is another thing to carry out the genocide of all its members and supporters, a solution which was not even applied to the nazis. europe must respond.

- mr president, i regret that my remarks are addressed to mr barnier, whom i particularly appreciate, but the complaints concern another commissioner. as a member of the committee on petitions, and to echo what mr perry has said, i must point out that it is not uncommon for the european commission to refuse to disclose to us the contents of documents or other information which is absolutely necessary to draw the right conclusions and to reach fair decisions in cases where european citizens turn to the european parliament. the commission's refusal to do so, in my view, violates the principle of transparency and makes our work in the committee on petitions more difficult. the main reason for the commission's negative attitude is that the information and documents requested would reveal sensitive personal data. the validity of this claim is disputed not only by the committee on petitions but also by the european ombudsman. i'll give you an example: the commission refuses to give us the names of representatives of a trade association which it has consulted and which appear to have influenced its decisions. we deny that this is protecting personal data and we fear that it is concealing a possible opaque transaction. mr perry also told you the story of lloyd's. i must tell you that behind this cover-up lies business ruin, misery for many people, even suicides. the report by mr gemelli, chairman of the committee on petitions, which was adopted by parliament today, therefore calls for the powers and efficiency of the committee on petitions to be increased so that citizens who make a complaint can be brought to justice in good time and irreparable damage to the environment or cultural monuments can be prevented.
counsel. - i will answer mr alavanos that the council is fully aware of the seriousness of the question he raises. i would remind him that, in general, article 152 - the former article 129 of the treaty on european union - states that community action in the field of human health complements national policies. the community shall encourage cooperation between member states in the fields covered by the treaty and, where necessary, support their action. in this context, it shall also ensure a high level of human health protection in the definition and implementation of all policies and actions. the fourth wto ministerial conference, which took place in doha from 9 to 14 november last, adopted, as you know, a declaration on the trips agreement and public health. in this declaration, the ministerial conference recognizes, on the one hand, the seriousness of the public health problems which affect many developing and least developed countries in particular and, on the other hand, the importance of the protection of intellectual property also for the development of new medicines. in the above-mentioned declaration, wto members agreed that the trips agreement does not prevent members from taking appropriate measures to protect public health and affirmed that the trips agreement can and should be interpreted and applied in a manner that upholds the right of all wto members to protect public health and, in particular, to promote access to all medicines. more specifically, the declaration includes among the flexibilities recognised in the trips agreement the right of each member to grant compulsory licences and to determine what constitutes a national emergency or an extreme emergency, with the understanding that public health crises, including those related to hiv/aids, tuberculosis, malaria and other epidemics, may constitute a national emergency or extreme emergency. in addition, it reaffirms the commitment of developed wto members to provide incentives to their companies and institutions to promote and encourage the transfer of technology to the least developed countries. the declaration also provides that least developed countries will not be obliged to apply or enforce the rights under sections v (patents) and vii (protection of undisclosed information) of part ii of the trips agreement with respect to pharmaceutical products until 1 january 2016, without prejudice to the right of least developed countries to request further extensions of the transition periods. i would add that in the same declaration the wto member states undertook to propose, by july 2002, real solutions that could be put into practice to solve the problem of developing and least-developed countries lacking the capacity to produce medicines. the problem is that parallel imports or other measures must be introduced to enable these countries to have access to these medicines at reasonable prices, at the lowest possible price. this will have to be done by next summer and i assure you that it will take a lot of hard work to get there. time is running out. the problem is extremely complex, but this is a very important commitment, because it corresponds to an aspect of the problem which could not be dealt with by the agreement.

- mr president, we fear that preventing conflict by non-military means is another aspect of the same coin, namely the european union's policy of intervention, which has turned itself into an international peacemaker, and indeed for crises and conflicts which it itself instigates, in order to intervene as a savior. the aim is to stifle the progressive popular movement and to promote the interests of european big business. the green lagendijk report is unfortunately not free from the spirit of intervention by political, economic and military means. there is no doubt that the right hand of the proposed civilian rapid reaction unit and the euro-police will be, as the rapporteur himself has acknowledged today, the euro-army, the preparation of which will be the task of the laeken summit the day after tomorrow. it has also been declared by the most responsible mouths of the european union that where the political proposal is not accepted, the military will prevail. for the above reasons, the kke members cannot vote in favour of the report... - mr. president, i just want to say that the events of september 11th have made us all wiser. they had a huge human cost, but they made us all wiser, and us and iran, i would say, and we understand the need, in such a vast, important and dangerous zone of instability, for bridges of contact, dialogue, discussion, cooperation between the european union and iran to begin to find their way. i think that both the troika and commissioner patten have done a very interesting job and i would say that both the report and the motion for a resolution are moving in the right direction overall. but i have to say, she's a little schizophrenic. i am afraid that the report contains a double message. it's like saying to a neighbor with whom you had a bad relationship, who you don't yet appreciate very much: "come sit at the same table with me, but know that you are vulgar, that you are uncouth, that you are a thief". that's kind of what we say in our introduction. in paragraph 19, for example, we are, i think, making a direct intervention inside iran, at a time when there is a tradition, a mistrust even among the most well-meaning people of iran, who look at the united states, great britain, the european union with suspicion that they will come to check again. in this sense, i think we should be particularly careful. we must be in favour of reforms. not for the reformers or against the conservatives. we have no right, as in any other country, but to set our own conditions, starting from the principles of the european union, european culture, as conditions for meeting and discussion, but not interference if hatami is good, if the clergy are bad. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. i think that the philosophy with which we have dealt and are dealing with the approach with turkey, which is today a candidate for membership of the european union, must also be embodied in our relations with iran. this does not mean, i repeat, that we should reduce the importance of fundamental issues of democratic human rights, women's rights, prisoners' rights, religious minorities' rights. if we are careful, i think we will take these steps, which will be important for both sides.
the rapporteur. - mr president, madam vice-president of the commission, the report we are discussing on the rapid withdrawal of single-hull tankers seemed at first an extremely complicated matter and, until we reached today's result, we had a long and difficult road ahead of us. i think that the commission has done a good job in this respect, and i hope that the council will do its best to ensure that the commission's proposals are implemented. on the one hand, we have been able to respond effectively to the concerns of the citizens of europe, particularly in france, following the sinking of the tanker erika, with regard to the protection of the environment. at the same time, we have taken substantial measures to ensure safety of navigation because, as is accepted by all parties, double-hull tankers are, at least in theory, without this being a panacea, safer than single-hull tankers. but we also hit another target. the aim of this is to protect the competitiveness of shipping. we must not forget that it is a vital sector of our economy, a sector which employs tens of thousands of workers across europe and it must be one of our priorities to protect this sector. we have also achieved something else, which i think is particularly useful to highlight: the european parliament has been able to find an international solution to a problem which transcends national frontiers. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted. i think that this is due to the european commission, the member states and the european parliament, because thanks to our initiative, thanks to our threat that we would go unilaterally if the international maritime organisation did not act, the international maritime organisation, which is true that it does not always operate at the speed that we all would like to operate, has gone ahead and adopted some measures which do not deviate from the original proposals that the commission and the member states had tabled. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted by the oil spills. on the one hand, in france, i think the citizens are relatively satisfied with the measures we are taking, all europeans who are concerned about the environment are satisfied with the significant progress being made, and on the other hand, the industry, the people involved in shipping, the seafarers and the shipowners, feel that we have not gone so far as to undermine the interests of this important sector of the economy. i would therefore like to thank all the colleagues with whom we have worked effectively to finally have this text before us and, of course, to thank and congratulate the vice-president of the european commission, mrs de palacio, for the way in which she has handled the matter, for her determination, but also for her goodwill towards the european parliament.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, as a member of the conciliation committee i am pleased that we have reached a positive result and that we can now proceed with the implementation of effective measures to protect our seas and our shipping. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's maritime transport policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new technologies, and that the community's maritime transport policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new technologies. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted by the introduction of new fishing vessels. the requirement of a double wall is also being gradually introduced and the rules on ship inspection and control bodies are being improved. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented in a timely manner, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report. let me also remind you and emphasize that my country, greece, whose economy, as we know, depends on shipping to a much greater extent than that of the other partners, is leading the way in the fight for safety at sea, in the effort to protect human life and the environment. i would also like to thank all those who have contributed to the successful outcome of this effort, in particular the commissioner, and i would like to remind you that the european seas are not a separate entity from the rest of the seas of our planet.
- mr president, in the lachine declaration there are words that satisfy everyone and have weight. the aim and the method, the assembly, the political negotiation, are things that satisfy. however, there are no convincing references in the declaration, as other colleagues have said, to the welfare state, which some people say is a shameful social model, to sustainable development, to sustainable development, to an autonomous and, i would say, therefore viable presence of the union. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. but it is important that we did not say anything serious about the war, we did not say anything serious about enlargement itself. i would like to make a point here, mr president. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. we're leaving a black hole in the balkans. it's unacceptable and i'd like an answer. i am afraid that i shall not be able to get it now and i am afraid that it will not be given to the assembly either.
- mr president, the reality is that the laeken summit has further strengthened a european union of big capital against the interests of workers and peoples in general. the people gathered in the palace of laeken did not hear the voice of the hundreds of thousands of demonstrators from all over europe: the poor, the unemployed, the jobless candidates, the creators of wealth, the victims of cruel exploitation. they have not heard and could not hear their anguish and their demand for another europe, for peace, for friendship between peoples, not for the euro-army, for intervention and war, for respect for and the development of social rights and democratic freedoms, not for the euro-mandate, eurojust and the terrorists who, on the pretext of 11 september, brand social and political struggles for a better tomorrow as terrorism. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the continuation and development of the unprecedented sea of mud that has appeared in laeken is, mr president, the only hope and the only way out.
- mr president, i will only refer, for the sake of time, to the truly historic decision to convene a constituent assembly, as already, but inaccurately, the 'convent' has been called. the european parliament, mr president, has every right to be proud because it was here, in our premises, that the idea of an assembly was conceived and took concrete form in reports of the european parliament, especially from 1997 onwards. the european parliament has consistently stressed that institutional developments are not possible without a change of draftsmen in the drafting of the new treaties, that is, without the role of the bureaucracy being taken over by a political body. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very important one. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council's proposal is to be submitted to the european parliament for approval. if instead of concrete proposals the assembly submits to the intergovernmental conference various alternatives, then the influence on the formulation of the new treaty will be minimal. unfortunately, at this point, lacken's mandate is more open-ended than it should be. if we consider that no institution in the european union or even an institution of a national state has studied and given its views on the institutional issues of the european union as much as the european parliament, then we must consider its participation with 16 representatives as not being commensurate with its offer so far. however, in order not to confuse our fellow citizens, the assembly will not be presenting a draft constitution, but a draft constitutional treaty. the intergovernmental method was not abolished, but was supplemented by the community method. this is reasonable, since the european union is both a union of peoples and a union of states. the european parliament is the next and new arena for the struggle for more democracy and the rule of law in the european union. it is a pity, mr president, that the bureau of the assembly was appointed by the council. in a house composed by a majority of members, it would be more appropriate to let it alone elect its bureau.
